or run, so every item is a reading of the code — **reproduce before fixing**, and drop an item
that does not reproduce rather than bending a test to fit it. Where a defect predates the branch
it says so; those are here because the branch touched the code or started depending on it.

### Writes to the user's files

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B302 ✅ | **"Exclude from auto-formatting" deletes a new file, and discards every uncommitted edit in a modified one** | Code Review / formatting | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | `CodeReview.razor.cs` ~554 reverts the class's file whenever `_isModelModified`, which is true for *any* working-copy status including `Untracked` and `Added`, and ignores the result. Git `RevertFiles` deletes `NewInWorkdir`/`NewInIndex` files and SVN deletes unversioned ones, so excluding a class in a file not yet committed destroys the file unrecoverably, and in a modified `package.mo` it throws away hand edits to every *other* class in it. The revert predates the branch; B253/B290 reworked the method around it. Never revert an untracked or added file; revert only when the file's other changes are known to be formatting, or ask first and name the file; check `VcsOperationResult.Success`. **Fixed 2026-09-25, awaiting a check by hand of the button itself**: `FormattingRevert.Decide` (MLQT.Services/Helpers) now allows the revert only when the file is `Modified`, the repository applies formatting, and the working copy is exactly what formatting the committed text produces (line endings aside); otherwise the annotation is still written and a snackbar says why the file was left alone. The revert runs under a `MonitorPause` and a failed revert stops the toggle with an error. `FormattingRevertTests` (15) hold the decision - untracked, added, an edit to another class, formatting off, CRLF, an unparseable committed version. The Code Review page has no bUnit harness, so the click path is unrun: exclude a class in a new untracked file (file must survive, annotated, with the snackbar), in a committed file with a hand edit to another class (the edit must survive), and in a file changed only by formatting (reverted, then annotated). **Checked by hand 2026-09-26**: the button behaves as the decision says - a new file survives and is annotated with the snackbar saying why it was not reverted, a hand edit to another class survives, and a file changed only by formatting is reverted and then annotated. |
| B303 ✅ | **Split into files deletes the source file even when some classes were not written** | Code Review / formatting | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | `ModelicaPackageSaver` catches and logs each failed `WriteAllText` and carries on, so the save "succeeds" with a partial `WrittenFiles`. `PackageSplitter` then asks `StillLivingIn(graph, currentFile, modelIds)` with the *requested* subtree, not what was written, so B243's guard counts a class whose write failed as moved and deletes the file (for a directory package, a failed rewrite of `package.mo` means it is not in `WrittenFiles` and the package's own `package.mo` is deleted). Triggers: disk full, a quoted identifier illegal as a file name. A throw mid-save returns `Failed` with no file list and leaves a partial tree beside `P.mo`, so the next load sees duplicate classes. Delete only when every class that lived in the file is in `ModelIdToFilePath`; report partial writes and what was left on disk. **Done 2026-09-25**: `SaveResult.FailedFiles` is new and a nested class is recorded only against a file actually written. The splitter snapshots the package's directory (a directory package's own `package.mo`/`package.order` as bytes) and, if any class is left unwritten, any write fails or the save throws, deletes what the split created, restores those files and reports the failure, listing anything it could not remove. Held by `IfAClassCannotBeWritten_TheFileItLivesInIsKept`, `APartialSplitIsUndone`, `IfThePackageFileCannotBeWritten_NothingIsDeleted` and `ADirectoryPackageThatCannotBeSplitWholeIsPutBackAsItWas`, all failing on the old code. |
| B304 ✅ | **"Declarations in order" reorders record fields, which silently changes what a positional record-constructor call binds** | Formatting / rules | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | `ModelicaRenderer.DeclarationGroupPasses` applies the five passes to every class whatever its restriction, and neither `DeclarationKinds.KindOf` nor `DeclarationOrder.CheckSection` exempts records. `record R Real b; parameter Real a = 1; end R;` is rewritten with `a` first, and every `R(2.0)` in the library now sets a different field without an error (Modelica §12.6 orders the constructor's inputs by declaration). The rule reports the same records, so a user fixing the finding is led into it. Functions are safe - inputs and outputs are one group. B252's gate checked formatter/rule agreement, not behaviour. Leave `record`/`operator record` in source order in both the renderer and the rule; add a rendering test on a record. **Done 2026-09-25**: `DeclarationKinds.KeepsSourceOrder(composition)` is the one answer for `record` and `operator record`, and both the renderer's `DeclarationGroupPasses(context)` and the rule ask it, so they still agree. Connectors need no exemption (members match by name; `AConnectorIsStillChecked`), and *Components before classes* writes components in one pass in source order. Held by `ARecordsFields_KeepTheirSourceOrder`, `ARecordNestedInAModel_KeepsItsOrder_WhileTheModelIsOrdered` and `DeclarationOrderTests.ARecordsFields_AreNotReported`. |
| B305 ✅ | **Split into files ignores the name-list exclusion and `ApplyFormattingRules`, and rewrites children already in their own files** | Code Review / formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | `CodeReview.razor.cs` ~1970 passes only `settings.ToFormattingOptions()` (which deliberately ignores `ApplyFormattingRules`) and `PackageSplitter` passes no `excludedModelIds`, where `FormattingPipeline` passes `FormattingExcludedModels`. A name-listed class is reordered (and with B304, a record's fields); a repository with formatting off has every moved class re-laid-out. `SubtreeOf` also includes children already in their own files, so they are rewritten too, contradicting "touches nothing else". Ask `FormattingExclusion.Excludes` per model in the saver rather than the annotation plus a separate list; with formatting off, move classes verbatim; leave already-separate children alone. No test covers either exclusion. The same shape as B284: fixed in one writing path, not its sibling. **Done 2026-09-25**: `SaveLibraryToDirectoryWithResult` takes the repository's `StyleCheckingSettings` instead of an id list, asks `FormattingExclusion.Excludes` per model, and moves every class verbatim when `ApplyFormattingRules` is off; `FormattingPipeline` and the split both pass it, and the split saves only the classes stored in the package's own file. Held by `AClassInTheNameListIsMovedAsItWasWritten`, `WithFormattingSwitchedOffEveryClassIsMovedAsItWasWritten`, `AChildAlreadyInItsOwnFileIsNotRewritten` and the control `AFormattedClassIsLaidOutAgain`. |
| B306 ✅ | **Splitting a single-file library (`MyLib.mo` at the repository root) leaves the graph and the library's `SourcePath` stale** | Code Review / libraries | ⭐⭐ | M | `SingleFilePackageAnalyzer` reports a top-level class in its own file, so the root can be split. For such a library `SourcePath` is the file, and `UpdateChangedFilesAsync(changed, library.SourcePath)` computes `Path.GetRelativePath(file, file)` = `"."` for the deleted file, which does not end `.mo`, so the old nodes are never removed and the new files resolve to `../MyLib/...`. Every later suppress/correct/exclude says "Cannot locate the file". All `PackageSplitterTests` load directory libraries. Refuse to split a file library's root, or re-register the library as a directory library after it. **Done 2026-09-25, by refusing**: `CodeReview.WhyNotSplit` declines, before the confirmation dialog, any library whose source is a single `.mo` file or an archive - judged by the path, since a repository library's `SourceType` is Git or SVN. Held by `CodeReviewSplitPackageTests.ALibraryLoadedFromOneFileIsNotSplitInPlace`, `ALibraryThatIsADirectoryCanBeSplit` and `AnArchiveIsNeverWritten`. Re-registering the library as a directory so the finding can be fixed in place is left undone. |
| B307 ✅ | **After a split the whole library is re-analysed and re-checked, and reported as "Revert analysis complete"** | Code Review / analysis | ⭐⭐ | S | `CodeReview.razor.cs` ~1997 fires `VcsModelsChanged(repository.Id, library.ModelIds.ToList())`, discarding the affected set `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` returned, so `MainLayout.OnVcsModelsChanged` drops every finding in the library and re-runs dependency analysis and style checking over all of it (minutes on MSL) under the revert message. No `NotifyFileActivity`, so the browser's VCS status and baseline do not see the new and deleted files; no `ModelContentChanged`, so the page re-shows the cached pre-split render. Pass the affected ids, and raise the two notifications `SaveAnnotatedFileAsync` already does. **Done 2026-09-25**: `CodeReview.AnnounceReloadedModels` raises `NotifyFileActivity`, `ModelContentChanged` and `VcsModelsChanged` with only the ids `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` returned - after an undone split too - and MainLayout's message no longer says "revert". Held by `ASplitAnnouncesTheClassesItReloadedAndNothingElse`, `ASplitIsFileActivity`, `NothingReloadedIsNothingToReanalyse` and `TheReloadAfterASplitNamesTheMovedClassesNotTheLibrary`. |
| B308 ✅ | **Files created by a split are UTF-8 with LF endings, whatever the file they came from** | Formatting | ⭐ | S | `ModelicaFileEncoding` applies a file's encoding and line endings only when the target already exists, so a Windows-1252 CRLF `P.mo` splits into UTF-8 LF files beside CRLF ones. Carry the source file's encoding and newline through to the files the split creates. **Done 2026-09-25**: `ModelicaFileEncoding.StyleOf` reads a file's encoding and line ending, and `WriteAllTextLike`/`WriteAllLinesLike` write an existing file in its own style and a new one in the style given; the splitter passes the source file's. Held by `TheNewFilesAreWrittenTheWayTheOldOneWas` (a Latin-1 CRLF package) and seven new `ModelicaFileEncodingTests`. |
| B309 ✅ | **`PackageCodeTrimmer` still carries its own copy of the "standalone child" rule B245 unified** | Checking / formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | `PackageCodeTrimmer.cs` ~80-92 groups by `Name.ToLowerInvariant()` and excludes `package`, "same rule as ModelicaPackageSaver" - but B245 moved the saver to `PackageFileLayout.StandaloneChildNames`, which compares differently and allows a package called `Package`. For case-differing pairs the saver writes a child as a file while the trimmer leaves it in the stored package text, and a package written verbatim (format=false or name-listed) then carries the child inline *and* beside it - a duplicate definition, a load error. Call `PackageFileLayout` from the trimmer, and make the verbatim path excise children it writes separately. **Done 2026-09-25**: The trimmer now calls `PackageFileLayout.StandaloneChildNames`, and the saver's `ExciseStandaloneChildrenFromVerbatimPackages` cuts standalone children out of a verbatim package's text through `PackageCodeTrimmer.ExciseInlineClasses` - by token position, keeping the text's line endings; a child that cannot be cut out without a neighbour stays inline and is not also written as a file. Held by `APackageAndAModelDifferingOnlyInCase_AreExcisedLikeTheSaverWritesThem`, `ExciseInlineClasses_KeepsTheTextsLineEndings_AndEverythingElseVerbatim` and three `AVerbatimPackage_*` saver tests. |
| B310 ✅ | **Every repository's committed `.mlqt/settings.json` is rewritten on load, and on every reorder** | Settings / repositories | ⭐⭐ | S | `SaveRepositorySettingsAsync` calls `StyleSettings.RecordDefaults()` (B244) and writes every non-reference repository's settings file, and it runs at the end of every repository load, on project change, add/remove and each B188 arrow click. The first launch after this branch adds `"MLQT.Structure.SingleFilePackage": "Warning"` to every repository's committed file with no user action - an unexplained modified file in each commit dialog, the "changes nobody made" shape of B236/B251. The file monitor ignores `.mlqt`, so the browser does not show it. `File.WriteAllText` with the default serializer may also flip its line endings between platforms (unverified). Record defaults only on an explicit Apply; skip the write when the serialized text is unchanged; save the project list alone for a reorder. **Done 2026-09-25**: Each repository keeps `Repository.SettingsOnDisk`, its settings as last read or written; a save writes only when they differ or there is no file yet, keeps the file's line endings and final newline, and is skipped when the text would be identical. `RecordDefaults` moved to the new `IRepositoryService.ApplyRepositorySettingsAsync(id)`, called by the Edit Repository dialog's Apply and MCP `set_style_settings`. A settings file that fails to parse is no longer overwritten with defaults. Held by `RepositorySettingsFileTests` (7). |
| B311 ✅ | **Line-ending detection misreads a UTF-16 file as LF** | Parser / encoding | ⭐ | S | `ModelicaFileEncoding.ExistingNewline` reads the bytes as Latin-1 and counts `\n` preceded by `\r`; in UTF-16LE CRLF is `0D 00 0A 00`, so every `\n` follows a `\0` and the file is rewritten as LF - B251 for that encoding, which `DetectFromBytes` otherwise supports. Count newlines on the decoded text. **Not reproduced 2026-09-25**: the `StreamReader` in `ExistingNewline` honours a byte-order mark before the Latin-1 it is given, and `DetectFromBytes` treats a file as UTF-16 only when it has one, so a UTF-16 CRLF file already counted as CRLF - by accident. The mark detection is now explicit and commented, and `ForFile_ReadsTheEndingsOfAUtf16File` (LE and BE) fails with it switched off. |

### MCP server and diagrams

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |

| B312 ✅ | **`remove_component` and `remove_connection` delete whatever precedes the statement on its line** | MCP | ⭐⭐ | S | `StructureEditTools.RemoveWholeLine` cuts from the start of the line to the semicolon without checking the text before `start` is whitespace. `Real x; Real y;` with `remove_component y` loses `x`; `x = 1; connect(a.y, b.u);` with `remove_connection` loses the equation. The result still parses, so the parse check passes and code is silently lost; B274's "original lines minus exactly one" is satisfied by exactly this deletion. Remove the whole line only when nothing but whitespace precedes the statement; otherwise remove the statement alone. Add a two-statements-on-one-line test for each tool. **Done 2026-09-25**: The whole line goes only when the statement is alone on it; otherwise only the statement and the blanks beside it go, and the neighbour keeps its indentation. Held by `StructureRemovalTests.RemovingAComponentThatSharesItsLineKeepsTheOtherStatement` and `RemovingAConnectionThatSharesItsLineKeepsTheOtherStatement`, with the statement before and after. |
| B313 ✅ | **The MCP format tools ignore `__MLQT(format=false)` and the name list, and cannot produce "Declarations in order"** | MCP / formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | `FormattingTools` renders through `RenderFileOwnerModel` with its own `FormattingOptions` and nothing in `MLQT.McpServer` calls `FormattingExclusion.Excludes`, so `format_class` reorders and rewrites a class that opted out (pre-existing; since B181/B252 it now reorders). It also has no `declarationOrder` option and passes no simple-type lookup, while `check_class`/`check_library` report `MLQT.Style.DeclarationOrder` - an agent sees findings no tool can clear, against `settings-reference.md`'s "the two always agree". Apply `FormattingExclusion` with the owning repository's settings; add the option and render through the same lookup the checker builds. **Done 2026-09-25**: `format_class` refuses a file holding any class excluded under the owning repository's settings (the new shared `McpServer/Helpers/RepositorySettings.cs`, which `StyleTools` now uses too). Both tools take `declarationOrder`, and `format_class` renders with `StyleChecking.CreateSimpleTypeLookup`, the lookup `check_class` gives the rule. Held by four new `QualityToolsTests`, including format-then-check agreement on a derived simple type. |
| B314 ✅ | **Connection lines are routed to where a connector sits on the diagram layer, but it is drawn where it sits on the icon layer - and the wrong points are written into files** | MCP / diagrams | ⭐⭐ | M | `DiagramImage` draws a component's connectors from `Layer.Icon` (`iconTransformation` first), while `DiagramGeometry.Locate` reads `Placements(ownerCode)` = `Layer.Diagram`, takes the type's *first* `coordinateSystem` (possibly the Diagram's), and rotates about the extent centre where the renderer rotates about `origin`. 157 MSL files use `iconTransformation`, e.g. `Blocks/package.mo` `RealOutput phi`. Lines end where no connector is drawn, and `ConnectionLineAnnotator` - shared by `add_connection` and `set_component_placement` - writes those wrong `Line(points=...)` into the user's file. Route through the icon layer and coordinate system the renderer uses, rotating about `RotationCentre`; test with a connector whose two placements differ. **Done 2026-09-25**: `DiagramSvgRenderer` exposes the transform it draws with (`ToParent`, `PortOf`, `PortOnEdge`) and `DiagramImage.ComponentOn` builds a component exactly as the image does; the router asks those two and works nothing out itself, so the Line points written by `add_connection` and `set_component_placement` are the drawn ones. Held by `DiagramRoutingTests` (a connector whose two placements differ, rotation about an off-centre origin, the Line written to the file) and new port tests in `DiagramSvgRendererTests`. |
| B315 ✅ | **Protected components vanished from `get_diagram_layout` and are not drawn, while lines are still routed to them** | MCP / diagrams | ⭐⭐ | S | `DiagramTools` and `DiagramImage` now call `ClassElementResolver.Collect(..., includeProtected: false, ...)`; `main` listed `ClassBodyLocator` components, protected included - a regression. `DiagramGeometry.Placements` still includes them, so `Modelica.Blocks.Examples.BusUsage` draws five lines to a `controlBus` that is not there (about 20 MSL protected sections hold placed components). Use `includeProtected: true` for a class's own diagram; keep `false` in `ConnectorsOn`. **Done 2026-09-25**: One `DiagramImage.Members` serves both tools and includes protected components, declared or inherited; `DiagramGeometry.Placements` includes inherited protected ones on the Diagram layer only, and a component's icon connectors stay public-only. Held by `DiagramPlacementTests.ProtectedComponentsAreOnTheClassesOwnDiagram`. |
| B316 ✅ | **Inherited connections, diagram graphics and coordinate system are not drawn** | MCP / diagrams | ⭐⭐ | M | Since B196 inherited components are drawn, but `DiagramImage` reads connections and `ExtractDiagram` from the class's own code only, and `IconExtractor` says diagrams are "not inherited the way an icon is" - Modelica inherits the Diagram layer (hence `DiagramMap`) and connect equations. For the common `extends PartialX` pattern the picture shows the base's components with no wires and no background, which an agent reads as unconnected. Collect connects and diagram graphics down the extends chain; correct the comment. **Done 2026-09-25**: `ClassElementResolver.BaseClasses` lists a class's bases in drawing order; connects are read from each base (types resolved in the base that declares them), the bases' Diagram layers are merged beneath the class's own, and the coordinate system comes from the most derived class that states one (`IconData.DeclaresExtent`). The `IconExtractor` comment is corrected. Held by `DiagramInheritanceTests` (5), `ClassElementResolverBaseClassesTests` and `DeclaresExtent_SaysWhetherTheExtentWasStated`. |
| B317 ✅ | **Parameter values are read with `GetText()`, which drops spaces, and `%parameter` labels then shorten component references** | MCP / diagrams / interface | ⭐⭐ | S | B277 moved only `Condition` to `SourceText`; `ClassInterfaceExtractor.ScalarModificationValue` and `ReadBinding` still use `GetText()`, so `y=pulse.y and step.y` becomes `pulse.yandstep.y`, which `IconText.Display` (last segment of a dotted identifier, meant for enumerations) shows as **y**. Also visible as `ifathen1else2` in `get_class_interface` defaults. Use `SourceText` in both (and `ExtractExtendsModifications`); restrict the last-segment rule to values that resolve to an enumeration literal or constant. **Done 2026-09-25**: `SourceText` rebuilds a value from its tokens with one space wherever the source had whitespace or a comment, and serves modification values, bindings, extends modifications and conditions. The last-segment rule applies only when the caller says a dotted value names a constant (`NamesAConstant`, answered by whether the prefix resolves to a class). Held by `Values_KeepTheSpacesBetweenTheirTokens`, `IconTextTests.AComponentReferenceIsShownWhole` and `DiagramLabelTests`; `AnArrayBinding_IsReadWhole` now expects `{1, 2, 3}` as written. |
| B318 ✅ | **`list_class_elements` on an encrypted-library class lists inherited members twice** | MCP / encrypted libraries | ⭐⭐ | S | For a stub `ViewTools` runs the resolver - which follows the stub's synthesized `extends` into any readable base such as `Interfaces.SISO` - and then appends `RecoveredInterface.ToElementViews`, which already lists inherited members. `u` and `y` come back twice and `count` is inflated; with `include_inherited=false` the documented inherited ones still appear. Merge by name, or take only the extends clauses from the resolver for a stub. **Done 2026-09-25**: The two lists are merged by name, keeping the documented member and taking `inheritedFrom` from the resolver, which is what `include_inherited=false` filters on. Held by `RecoveredInterfaceTests.AMemberInheritedFromAReadableBaseIsListedOnce_AndSaysWhereItCameFrom`. |

A block flush with the canvas edge loses its port triangles. Include mapped child extents and grow about the rotation centre. **Done 2026-09-25**: The view is measured through the frame each component is drawn with - the icon system's corners and each connector's, turned about the origin - with a placeholder measured as the unturned box it is drawn as. Held by four new `DiagramSvgRendererTests`, each failing against the old renderer. |
| B320 ✅ | **Text primitives ignore their `origin` and `rotation`** | Icons / diagrams | ⭐ | S | `IconSvgRenderer` computes `GetTransformAttribute(text.Origin, text.Rotation)` and then emits only the counter-flip `scale(...)`, so 43 MSL texts using `origin=` (e.g. `SpringDamper` in B277's PID_Controller) are misplaced and rotated labels are not rotated - in the desktop tree icons as well as the diagrams. Pre-existing; the branch rewrote the line. Emit the origin/rotation transform before the counter-flip. **Done 2026-09-25**: The origin/rotation transform is emitted before the counter-flip. Held by `RenderToSvg_TextWithOriginAndRotation_IsPlacedAndTurnedBeforeItIsRighted`, with mirrored and no-origin cases. |

Replace only the `transformation(...)` argument. **Done 2026-09-25**: Only the `transformation(...)` argument is replaced, or added first when the Placement has only an `iconTransformation`; the old origin and rotation go, as the new extent is absolute. Start from `NothingEnabled()`, or change the documented contract. **Done 2026-09-25, keeping the contract**: `ToSettings()` starts from `StyleCheckingSettings.NothingEnabled()`, as does `check_style`'s fallback with no settings and no repository - an explicit rule set names the rules, and a later default should not change an agent's results. Held by `ToSettings_TreatsAnUnmentionedDefaultOnRuleAsOffToo` (every default-on rule) and `ToSettings_StillSwitchesOnADefaultOnRuleThatIsAskedFor`. |
| B323 ✅ | **`VcsTools` tests "is this path inside the repository?" with a bare `StartsWith`** | MCP / revision control | ⭐ | S | `VcsTools.cs` ~146 `canon.StartsWith(localCanon)` has no separator, so `…/Lib` matches `…/LibExtra` - B274/B276's shape, likelier since B301 made shared working copies first-class. Use the separator-aware containment helper. **Done 2026-09-25**: The same defect was in `VcsChangeResolver.FormattableModelicaFiles`, which let the formatter rewrite files in such a sibling library. `GraphBuilder`'s correct private copy is now the public `ModelicaGraph.PathContainment.IsWithin`, used by all three. Held by `GetChangedClasses_ASiblingWithTheSamePrefixIsNotInTheRepository`, `AFileInASiblingWhoseNameStartsWithTheLibrarys_IsNotFormattable` and `PathContainmentTests`. **Follow-up 2026-09-25**: `LibraryDataService.ReloadFileAsync` and `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` assigned a file with no classes in the graph yet to a library by a bare `StartsWith`, so with `Lib` loaded first a class added to `LibExtra` was indexed under `Lib`. Both use one `LibraryContainingPath` over `PathContainment.IsWithin`, preferring the nearest root. Held by `ReloadFileAsync_ANewFile_BelongsToItsOwnLibrary_NotOneWhosePathIsAPrefix` and its `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` twin. |

### Revision control and the VCS pipeline

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B324 ✅ | **Push and Force Push use a stored branch name that is stale for a sibling repository - a force push can rewind the wrong remote branch** | Git | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | `PushAsync`/`ForcePushAsync` push `repository.CurrentBranch`, and Git pushes the branch it is given, not HEAD. `CreateBranchAsync` (switch defaults on) and `CommitAsync` refresh revision info for their own repository only, and B296 made Create Branch reload nothing - so for two libraries in one checkout, after Create Branch `feature` from A, Push from B runs `git push origin main`, and Force Push after a rebase from B runs `push --force-with-lease origin main`, which passes the lease against a freshly fetched remote and rewinds it. A switch made in another tool leaves it stale the same way. Push the actual HEAD branch (refuse on detached HEAD); refresh revision info across `GetRepositoriesSharingWorkingCopy` after Create Branch and Commit. **Done 2026-09-25**: The service names no branch any more: the Git layer's `TryResolvePushTarget` reads HEAD when the push runs and refuses on a detached HEAD, and Commit, Create Branch and both pushes refresh every repository sharing the working copy. Held by `SharedWorkingCopyTests.PushAndForcePush_PushTheBranchHeadIsOn_NotTheStoredOne`, `CreateBranchFromOneLibrary_MovesTheOtherLibrarysBranchToo`, `CommitFromOneLibrary_MovesTheOtherLibrarysRevisionToo` and `Push_OnADetachedHead_IsRefusedAndTouchesNothing`, which failed on the old code. |
| B325 ✅ | **Pending file changes are recorded against whichever repository enumerates last, and B301's per-repository pipeline acts on them** | File monitor / VCS pipeline | ⭐⭐ | M | `FileMonitoringService` fans each event out to every subscriber of a shared watch path, but `AddOrUpdateChange` deduplicates on the path alone, so each edit ends up under *one* repository, not the owner. Refresh groups by `RepositoryId` and formats library B's file with repository A's settings (and A's exclusion list); after a VCS operation pipeline A's formatter writes are recorded as B's pending changes, pipeline B formats A's files with B's settings and, because pending changes answered, never asks VCS status for B's own. Revert clears only its own repository's entries. `SharedWatchPathTests` says each gets its own record and asserts nothing about it. Key pending changes by repository and path, hand a repository only changes under its own `LocalPath`, and make the pipeline's stop/start cover the working-copy group as `MonitorPause` does. **Done 2026-09-25**: Records are keyed by (repository, path); each repository's `LocalPath` is registered with `SetRepositoryScope` (kept across a pause), and a change is recorded only for the repository whose scope holds it - the deepest when scopes nest - while file activity still reaches every subscriber. The VCS pipeline pauses the other repositories in the working copy while it formats, and Refresh pauses every repository in each affected working copy, restarting them after an exception too. Held by new `SharedWatchPathTests` cases; `ClearingOneRepositorysChanges_LeavesTheOthers` now checks the records. The MainLayout half has no direct test, as nothing renders MainLayout. |
| B326 ✅ | **A VCS operation can start while the previous operation's analysis pipeline is still formatting** | Library Browser / VCS pipeline | ⭐⭐ | M | `_vcsBusy` clears when `ReloadAndAnalyseAsync` returns; the pipeline runs detached behind `_vcsPipelineGate`. It calls `StopMonitoring`, so `MonitorPause.Begin` skips the repository, and its `finally` restarts monitoring mid-checkout. Switch Branch during "Applying code formatting…" reloads libraries under a running analysis and lets the formatter write old-branch text over the checkout (it reads, then writes file by file). Revert's analysis, Refresh and Checkout Revision are not behind the gate either. B294 deferred this to B296, which did not close it. Hold the busy state, or a shared VCS gate, until the pipelines an operation started have finished. **Done 2026-09-25**: AppState counts VCS work (`BeginVcsWork`, `IsVcsWorkInProgress`, `OnVcsWorkChanged`), and `VcsPipelineQueue` replaces MainLayout's gate, counting the work from the moment a pipeline is queued until it finishes - the revert's analysis included. While any runs, every browser's VCS actions, the history checkout and Refresh are disabled or refused. Held by `VcsPipelineQueueTests` and `LibraryBrowserUpdateTests.WhileThePipelineAnUpdateStartedRuns_NoOtherOperationCanStart`. |
| B327 ✅ | **Commit, Merge, Rebase and Push are offered on a detached HEAD with no guard** | Git | ⭐⭐ | S | B193 made detached HEAD reachable and warns that a commit there "belongs to nothing", but Commit is gated only on uncommitted changes, `GitRevisionControlSystem.Commit` has no `IsHeadDetached` check (only `UpdateToLatest` has), Merge/Rebase build messages from `"unknown"`, and the next Switch Branch orphans any commits made there silently. Disable or warn while `DetachedHeadLabel` is set, offer "create a branch here", and warn before switching away from unreachable commits. **Done 2026-09-25**: The Git layer refuses Commit, MergeBranch and Rebase on a detached HEAD (saying to continue or abort when a rebase is the cause); the browser disables Commit, Merge, Rebase, Push and Create pull request there and offers "Create a branch here"; `CountCommitsOnNoBranch` lets Switch Branch warn with how many commits it would strand. Held by `GitOperationsTests`' detached-HEAD region, `LibraryBrowserDetachedHeadTests` and `SwitchBranchDialogStrandedCommitsTests`. |
| B328 ✅ | **SVN history content is fetched with no peg revision, so a file deleted or moved since cannot be shown** | SVN | ⭐⭐ | S | `SvnCli.RunForBytes("cat", "-r", revision, url)` with a plain URL pegs at HEAD, so `svn cat -r N` fails for a path that no longer exists; the local route pegs `@HEAD` too. B265's note claims the server fallback also fixes "a file deleted since"; only the still-existing case was verified. Peg the URL at the operative revision (escaping a literal `@`), with a URL-shape test beside the `ContentUrls` tests. **Done 2026-09-25**: The server fallback's URL is pegged at the revision asked for through a new `Pegged` helper, and the BASE route at BASE, so a file name holding '@' is not read as a peg. Held by `SvnIntegrationTests.GetFileBytesAtRevision_FindsAFileDeletedSince`, which failed against the old line, and two `SubversionContentUrlTests`. |
| B329 ✅ | **SVN update, switch and create-branch discard svn's own message** | SVN | ⭐⭐ | S | `SvnRevisionControlSystem` reports `"SVN update failed."`, `"SVN switch failed."` and `"Failed to create branch."` without `StdErr`, where commit, merge and resolve surface it - so B297's "reported as a failure that says so" never reaches the user for the commands most likely to stall, nor do authentication errors or svn's "run 'svn cleanup'". Use the `StdErr`-or-default pattern. **Done 2026-09-25**: `SvnCli.Result.FailureMessage(fallback)` is the one "stderr, or this fallback" rule, used by all six SVN operations. Held by `SvnCliTests.FailureMessage_*` and `SvnIntegrationTests.UpdateToLatest_WhenSvnRefuses_ReportsSvnsOwnMessage`. |
| B330 ✅ | **A timed-out git or svn command is killed hard and leaves its lock behind, with nothing to detect or clear it** | Revision control | ⭐⭐ | M | `Kill(entireProcessTree: true)` is TerminateProcess/SIGKILL, so a killed `update-index --refresh` (B298, in every slow status) leaves `.git/index.lock` and a killed `svn update`/`switch` (B297's stalled-server case) leaves the working copy locked; nothing in the repository mentions `index.lock`, `svn cleanup` or E155004, so every later operation fails with the tool's lock error (hidden for SVN by B329). Also: B293's single-flight is per repository id, so two repositories on one checkout run two status scans that race for the index lock. Remove a lock the killed process created, or tell the user how; make status single-flight per `VcsRootPath`. **Fixed 2026-09-25, the svn half awaiting a check by hand**: After a kill `RunGitCommand` removes the `*.lock` files in the git directory and `refs/` written since the command started, and says what it removed or what the user must delete. SVN results carry `Stopped`, and `SvnCli.RunOnWorkingCopy` - used by update, switch, commit, merge, revert and resolve - follows a stopped command with `svn cleanup` and reports how that went. `GetWorkingCopyChanges` caches and single-flights per working copy rather than per repository id. Held by `GitCommandRunnerTests.ALockLeftByAStoppedGit_IsRemoved_AndAnOlderOneIsNot`, `SvnCliTests.RunOnWorkingCopy_*` and `WorkingCopyQuerySharingTests`. The cleanup is tested with a fake runner only: point a working copy at a server that accepts and stalls (or lower `SvnCli.IdleTimeout`), run Update, and confirm the error mentions `svn cleanup` and a second Update does not fail with E155004. **Checked by hand 2026-09-25, with one more fix**: against svnserve behind a proxy that stops answering, the update was stopped at the idle limit and `svn cleanup` released the lock, as logged. But the browser then asks the server for the new revision's description before it reloads, and a server that stalled the update stalls that too - a second ten minutes of progress bar after the failure had been reported. That label query now has its own 30-second limit (`SvnCli.QueryIdleTimeout`, through new `Run`/`RunXml` overloads taking the limit). Held by `SvnIntegrationTests.AQueryOfAServerThatNeverAnswers_IsStoppedAtItsOwnLimit` (a real `svn.exe` against a listener that never answers) and `TheRevisionLabelsLimit_IsFarShorterThanAnUpdates`. Still to see by hand: the second Update succeeding once the proxy forwards again. **Checked by hand 2026-09-26, complete**: with the proxy stalling, Update is stopped, the error names `svn cleanup` and the progress bar clears once the 30-second label query gives up; with the proxy forwarding again, the next Update succeeds with no E155004. The read-only queries gained their own limits along the way - 30 seconds for small questions, 2 minutes for history walks - described in `svn-operations.md`. |

### External tools

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B331 ✅ | **The next Dymola check after a timeout or Stop kills Dymola - or starts a second one on the same port** | External tools | ⭐⭐⭐ | M | `DymolaInterfaceFactory.GetOrCreateAsync` asks `IsAliveAsync`, which returns false once the session is forced offline and otherwise pings with a 2 s timeout - a Dymola still busy with the abandoned check does not answer - and then `Dispose()`s the instance, which runs `_dymolaProcess?.Kill()`. So the check after a timeout or Stop kills the Dymola window MLQT started, with whatever the user had open, which B260 says must never happen; against a Dymola MLQT only attached to, it starts a second `dymola.exe -serverport` on the taken port. `external-tools.md` promises the next check simply waits. Tell *gone* (refused, process exited) from *busy* (accepts, does not answer) - `Probe()` already can - and never dispose or kill a busy session. The factory is outside the contract tests. **Fixed 2026-09-25, awaiting a check by hand against a live Dymola**: `DymolaInterface.GetSessionStateAsync()` answers Answering / Busy / Starting / Gone, busy being a connection accepted and not answered. The factory hands a busy session back with its commands let through to wait, waits for one still starting, never starts a Dymola when it finds a busy one on the port, and detaches every session before disposing it, so it can no longer end a Dymola. Sessions are built through `Func<DymolaSettings, IDymolaSession>`, and `MLQT.Services.Tests/DymolaInterfaceFactoryTests` (run by CI) holds this; `CommandTimeoutTests` covers the probe against a busy socket. To check: set a 5 s limit, time out a large model's check and check again at once - the window must stay open with one `dymola.exe`, and the second check waits or says Dymola is still busy; repeat after Stop; then start Dymola yourself with `-serverport 8082`, make it busy, and check from MLQT - it must attach, not launch another. **Checked by hand 2026-09-25**: a timed-out check followed at once by another, and a check straight after Stop, both wait for Dymola and leave its window open with one `dymola.exe`. Attaching to a Dymola the user started works, and the factory now logs each decision - state found, attached, started or let go, with process ids - under `DymolaInterfaceFactory`. **What it cannot do**: a Dymola running a command from its own window still answers its server, whether started with `-serverport` or `startHttpServer`, so MLQT cannot see it is busy; Dymola then interrupts that command to run MLQT's and closes once MLQT's check finishes. That is Dymola's behaviour, worth reporting to Dassault; `external-tools.md` now tells users not to work in the Dymola MLQT checks with while a check runs, or to give MLQT one of its own on another port. A second Dymola appeared once but did not reproduce with the user's Dymola confirmed listening on 127.0.0.1:8082 - most likely it was not yet listening then. |
| B332 ✅ | **A tool that will not start is reported as "<Tool> checked nothing." in a success-coloured alert** | External tools | ⭐⭐ | S | Both services call the factory's `GetOrCreateAsync` in `RunCheckingAsync` outside `EnsureLibraryLoadedAsync`'s handling, so a wrong `OmcPath`, a start timeout, "Dymola path not specified" or "did not start within the expected time" lands in the outer catch, which raises only `OnCheckingComplete`. The reason is in the log only. The contract test `AToolThatWillNotStartIsReportedRatherThanThrown` goes through `EnsureLibraryLoadedAsync`, not the path the UI uses. Produce a `ModelCheckResult` carrying the message, with a contract test on `StartCheckingAsync`. **Done 2026-09-25**: The run now ends with one `ModelCheckResult` from the shared `UnavailableTool.CouldNotStart`, carrying the exception's message and marked `ToolUnavailable`. The contract test `AToolThatWillNotStartEndsTheRunWithTheReason` holds it on the `StartCheckingAsync` path, for both tools. |
| B333 ✅ | **The UI still reports a timeout as a problem with the model** | External tools / Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | B263 promises a timeout is `TimedOut`, never a failed model, but `CodeReview.OnModelChecked` turns every `!Success` result into an Error `LogMessage` against the class, `FailedChecks` counts it, and the summary ("Dymola reported a problem with it.") has no timed-out state and does not say a package run stopped early. A library that times out opening is reported `"Failed to load library"` with `TimedOut` false in both services. Give the summary a timed-out/stopped state, keep timeouts out of the findings (or give them their own severity), and set `TimedOut` on a load timeout. **Done 2026-09-25**: The load step returns a shared `LibraryLoad` that knows whether it timed out; findings are filed only for `ModelCheckResult.IsModelFailure`; the headline says what ended a run early ("ran out of time on X and stopped there", "was not available", "stopped answering after N classes"), and the alert is green only for a complete clean run. Held by the contract tests `ALibraryThatRunsOutOfTimeToOpen…` and `CheckOutcomeSummaryTests`. |
| B334 ✅ | **If the tool dies partway through a package, every remaining class is a failed model with an empty message** | External tools | ⭐⭐ | S | `DymolaCheckingService.CheckSingleModelAsync` handles only `Cancelled` and `TimedOut`; a closed Dymola window gives `Failed` (offline is not set mid-session), `GetLastErrorAsync` returns "", and each remaining class becomes an empty "Dymola Check Failed" after a connect retry. If omc exits mid-run, `Exchange` waits out the whole time limit and blames it ("raise Check time limit"), or with a limit of 0 waits until Stop. End the run with a "the tool has gone" result on `Failed`/`Offline` or an exited process; the fakes cannot model those outcomes yet. **Fixed 2026-09-25, the Dymola half awaiting a check by hand**: After a Failed or Offline outcome Dymola asks the session's state and, only when it is Gone, ends the run with `UnavailableTool.WentAway`; omc's `Exchange` watches the process and throws `OpenModelicaExitedException`. The fakes can model a tool that dies. Held by `AToolThatGoesAwayMidPackageEndsTheRunSayingSo`, `AFailedCommandFromADymolaStillAnsweringDoesNotEndTheRun` and the live `OmcExitingUnderACommand_EndsTheWaitAndSaysSo`. To check: close Dymola's window partway through a package check - the run should end "Dymola stopped answering…" with no empty failures and nothing in findings, and the next check should start a new Dymola. **Checked by hand 2026-09-25**: closing Dymola's window partway through a package check ends the run saying Dymola stopped answering, with no empty failures and nothing filed as a finding, and the next check starts a new Dymola. |
| B335 ✅ | **Stop does nothing while the tool is starting, and a check started meanwhile is dropped or mixed into the other tool's** | External tools / Code Review | ⭐ | M | `GetOrCreateAsync` takes no token and can last ~60 s for Dymola. Stop closes the dialog but the run goes on; clicking the same tool again resets `_checkResults` and opens a dialog that `StartCheckingAsync` ignores (`_isRunning`), which the old run then closes with "stopped after 0 classes"; clicking the other tool lets the cancelled run's `OnCheckingComplete` close the other tool's dialog and dispose its token, leaving that run unstoppable. Pass the token into the factory, disable both check buttons while either service runs, and ignore events from the service that is not current. **Done 2026-09-25**: Both factory interfaces take a `CancellationToken`, carried through session construction, the process start and the standard-library load; a shared `CheckRunGate` ends a run *before* `OnCheckingComplete` is raised (a `finally` used to clear the flag after, and could clear a newer run's); `CodeReview` drives both tools through `MLQT.Shared/Helpers/ExternalCheckSession`, which disables both buttons while either runs and drops events from the tool that is not current. Held by `StopReachesARunThatIsStillStartingTheTool`, `ARunHasEndedByTheTimeItSaysItHasFinished`, `ACheckStartedFromTheCompletionHandlerIsNotRefused` and `ExternalCheckSessionTests`. |
| B336 ✅ | **Changing a tool's path or port does not reach a session already running** | External tools | ⭐ | S | Both factories' `UpdateSettings` replace only their settings object; `GetOrCreateAsync` returns the cached live instance and re-applies only `CommandTimeout`, and `ResetAsync` has no production caller. A new Dymola version, path or either port has no effect until the session dies or MLQT restarts - B263's "the path and port set in the tab never reached omc" is only half closed. Rebuild when path or port differ from the running instance. Tidy with it: the unused `error` in `OpenModelicaCheckingService.EnsureLibraryLoadedAsync`. **Done 2026-09-25**: Each factory copies the path and port its session was built for (the settings dialog edits the object in place) and rebuilds when they change - the old omc ended, the old Dymola detached, never killed. Both tools now drain the log before retrying a refused open and report the tool's own reason for the second refusal (`LibraryLoad.WouldNotOpen`), which retires the unused variable. Held by `DymolaInterfaceFactoryTests`, the live `TheFactoryStartsANewSessionWhenThePortChanges` and `ALibraryThatWillNotOpenCarriesTheToolsOwnReason`. |
| B337 ✅ | **Dymola auto-detection misses the spring releases, and B286's documentation is half right** | External tools / docs | ⭐ | S | `DymolaSettings` probes only `Dymola {year}x` and `Dymola {year}x Refresh 1`, so `Dymola 2022`, `Dymola 2023` (no x) are never found; and because `year--` runs before the `year > 2020` test, `Dymola 2020x Refresh 1` *is* probed, so "2021 onwards" in `external-tools.md` is wrong the other way too. Probe `Dymola {year}` as well and state exactly which install names are found. **Done 2026-09-25**: `DymolaSettings.CandidateInstallNames`/`FindInstalledDymola` probe, for each year from next year back to 2021, `Dymola {year}x Refresh 1`, `Dymola {year}x` and `Dymola {year}` - their release order - and `external-tools.md` names exactly those. Held by `DymolaInstallDetectionTests`. |
| B338 ✅ | **OpenModelica cannot be configured by browsing or auto-detection on Linux** | External tools / Linux | ⭐ | S | `BrowseForOpenModelicaFolder` always writes `bin/omc.exe`, and `OpenModelicaSettings` auto-detection probes only `Program Files` and `omc.exe` on PATH, so on the Linux host Browse produces a path that does not exist and nothing is found; `external-tools.md` has no Linux guidance. Use the platform's executable name and probe `/usr/bin/omc` and PATH on Linux; document it. **Fixed 2026-09-25, awaiting a check on a real Linux machine**: `OpenModelicaSettings.CandidateOmcPaths`/`FindInstalledOmc` check the versioned Program Files folders then `omc.exe` on PATH on Windows, and `/usr/bin/omc`, `/usr/local/bin/omc`, `/opt/openmodelica/bin/omc` then `omc` on PATH on Linux; Browse uses `OmcUnder(folder)`, which picks the platform's name and accepts the `bin` folder itself. `external-tools.md` has an "OpenModelica on Linux" section. Held by `OpenModelicaInstallDetectionTests`, parameterised by platform. **Changed 2026-09-27 after a check on Linux**: asking for `/usr` to reach `/usr/bin/omc` was the wrong question, so on Linux Browse now picks `omc` itself - a file picker opening in `/usr/bin`, unfiltered because a Linux program has no extension (`IFilePickerService.PickExecutableAsync`, new) - and the path is taken as chosen. Windows still picks the installation folder and takes `bin\omc.exe` inside it. `SettingsExternalTools.BrowseForOpenModelica(windows)` takes the platform, so `SettingsExternalToolsTests` holds both branches on either OS. To check on Linux: Browse opens a file dialog in `/usr/bin` showing `omc`, and choosing it fills the field with `/usr/bin/omc` and no warning. **Also 2026-09-27**: detection ran only in the constructor, before the saved values were applied, so a saved blank path overwrote what it found and clearing the field turned detection off for good. `OpenModelicaSettings` now implements `IJsonOnDeserialized` and looks again when the loaded path is blank, and an **Auto-detect** button beside the path runs the search on demand, blanking the field when nothing is found. Also to check on Linux: clear the path, save, restart - it comes back as `/usr/bin/omc`; Auto-detect over a wrong path replaces it. **Checked on Linux 2026-09-27, complete**: Browse opens a file dialog in `/usr/bin` and choosing `omc` fills `/usr/bin/omc`; a cleared path comes back as `/usr/bin/omc` after a restart; Auto-detect replaces a wrong path. |

### The Code Review page

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B339 ✅ | **Clicking a finding in a trimmed package scrolls to the wrong line** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | Findings for a trimmed package are counted against the trimmed text (`TrimElision`), but the viewer shows the untrimmed file slice and maps only through its own elision (`_elision.ToDisplayLine(pending)`); nothing in `MLQT.Shared` reads `TrimElision`, which the CLI/SARIF path does use through `ClassLocation`. In any `package.mo` with inline standalone classes, a finding after the first one lands on the wrong line. Map through `TrimElision.ToSourceLine` first; add a test. **Done 2026-09-25**: `CodeReview.DisplayLineOfFinding` maps a finding's line back through `ModelNode.TrimElision` first, as `ClassLocation` does for the CLI, and only then through the viewer's elision. Held by `CodeReviewFindingLineTests`, with nested classes hidden and shown, failing with the trim map removed. |
| B340 ✅ | **Code search corrupts the displayed code** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | `CodeViewer` HTML-encodes, inserts misspelling `<span class="code-misspell" data-word=…>` markup, and then runs the search `Replace` over the result. Typing `q`, `u`, `o` or `t` - the first keystroke of many searches, the box being `Immediate` - splits every `&quot;`, so each quote shows as literal text; `lt`, `gt`, `amp` break documentation; searching `class`, `span`, `data` or `word` in a class with spelling findings breaks the tag. Match on the decoded text and wrap around entities and tags; test with both. **Done 2026-09-25**: Misspellings and search matches are both found on the token's raw text and the spans placed around encoded pieces - nesting where a match crosses a misspelled word's edge - and untagged text is searched too. The page's line search reads the same `CodeViewer.VisibleText`, which decodes only text outside tags. Held by `CodeViewerHtmlTests.SearchingNeverChangesTheTextOnScreen`: 14 terms over quotes, `&lt;`, `&amp;` and misspelling spans, 13 of which failed against the old code. |
| B341 ✅ | **The diff view re-parses both sides and re-runs the diff on the UI thread on every render of the page** | Code Review / performance | ⭐⭐ | M | `DiffViewer.OnParametersSet` calls `ComputeDiff()` unconditionally - an O(m·n) LCS plus a full `ModelicaTokenClassifier.Highlight` parse per side - and the page re-renders for every findings batch, baseline change and progress update, so during a background check each render costs two parses; B185's lexer-first paint has no equivalent here. Memoise on content and mode, and compute off the dispatcher. **Done 2026-09-25**: The expensive half is the static `DiffViewer.Prepare`, keyed on the two texts and whether they are Modelica, run on the pool and producing finished HTML per side, with a generation counter so a superseded run cannot land; a mode or context change only re-lays-out the prepared diff, and is skipped when nothing it depends on changed. "Comparing…" shows meanwhile. Held by `DiffViewerMemoTests`, failing with the memo disabled; `DiffViewerPaneSyncTests` now waits for the after-render call the diff makes a render later. |
| B342 ✅ | **B185's early lexer paint consumes pending scrolls and search against the wrong document** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | For a class over 64 KB the quick paint sets `_highlightedCode`, empties the elision and clears loading, and `OnAfterRenderAsync` then consumes `_pendingScrollLine`, `_pendingScroll` and `_pendingScrollWord` against it; matches are not recomputed. A clicked finding scrolls in the unelided text and the page then swaps to the elided one; the search count still describes the previous class; hidden annotations flash visible. Hold pending scrolls until the tree render lands and recompute matches on the quick paint. **Done 2026-09-25**: The first paint re-finds the search, and pending finding, word and restore scrolls wait for the full render (`ReadyForPendingScrolls`). Held by `PendingScrollsWaitForTheRenderThatFollowsTheFirstPaint` and `TheSearchIsCountedAgainstTheFirstPaintNotThePreviousClass`, each failing with its part removed. Hidden annotations and nested classes still show during the first paint, since hiding them needs the tree. |
| B343 ✅ | **A trimmed package, and the diff's working-copy side, are sliced from the file as it is now with offsets from load time** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | `ClassSource.For` slices `ReadAllTextOnly(path)` at the model's `StartIndex`/`StopIndex` and returns null only past the end, for both the view and `WorkingCopyText`. After an external edit the monitor holds until Refresh, and meanwhile the package shows an arbitrary chunk of the new file, possibly mid-token, and the diff compares it with HEAD. Check that the trimmed slice reproduces the stored text and fall back to the stored text when it does not. **Done 2026-09-25**: The slice is checked before it is shown: a trimmed package still matching its file must give back exactly its stored text once the trimmed lines are dropped, and text something rewrote must name the class on its first line and, if it ends `end X`, end that class; otherwise the stored text is shown. Held by three new `ClassSourceTests`, the two edited-file ones failing without the check. |
| B344 ✅ | **The HEAD side of a diff can be a different class of the same short name, or the previous class** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | `headModels.FirstOrDefault(m => m.Name == ...Definition.Name)` matches on the short name, so in a file with several nested classes of one name (MSL `Media/package.mo`, `setState_pTX`) the diff is against the wrong one - predates the branch; B217 fixed only the working-copy side. The load lambda also reads `_currentModelNode` directly, so switching class mid-load writes the old class's HEAD into `_originalModelCode` after it was cleared, and the next `SetViewMode` skips the reload. Match by full name within the file, and capture the node before the task. **Fixed 2026-09-25, the mid-load half awaiting a check by hand**: `CodeReview.HeadSideOf` matches by full name within the file, falling back to the short name only when the class has moved and that name is unambiguous; `LoadModelDiffAsync` captures the node, repository and path before the task, applies the result only if that node is still shown, and only the latest load clears the spinner. Held by `TheHeadSideIsTheClassOfTheSameFullName` (the `Media`/`setState` shape). To check the race: open a modified class in a large file in diff mode, select another modified class while the spinner is up - its diff must be its own, and Single → Diff must still load. **Checked by hand 2026-09-27, complete**: against a 7.5 MB single-file library whose HEAD side takes about 3 s to parse, with one edit in `Dissipation3...kc_evenGapLaminar` and another in `Dissipation9...kc_evenGapTurbulent`, selecting either while the other's diff was loading showed the selected class's own diff, both ways round, and Single → Diff loaded again afterwards. The first attempt put the test comments between a class name and its description string and found B409 and B410 instead. |
| B345 ✅ | **A stale render can overwrite the current one when annotations are toggled mid-render** | Code Review | ⭐ | S | The render continuation checks only `NavState.ModelID`, not the annotation state; with annotations hidden the render parses twice, so toggling Show during a slow Hide render lets the Hide render land last - elided text with the button showing everything. Compare the cache key or a generation counter. **Done 2026-09-25**: Every render carries a generation and lands only if no later one was asked for (`TryApplyRender`); a superseded result is still cached, being right for its key. Held by `CodeReviewRenderLandingTests.ARenderAskedForEarlierDoesNotLandOverOneAskedForSince`, failing without the check. |
| B346 ✅ | **In diff mode, search, finding scroll and the annotation toggle do nothing useful** | Code Review | ⭐ | S | The find box stays enabled and counts matches in the single view's text; `ScrollToCurrentMatchAsync` targets `.code-viewer`, which is not rendered; `_pendingScrollLine` is consumed and lost. `ToggleAnnotations` still nulls and reloads the HEAD diff though B285 decided the diff always shows everything, so the diff loads twice. Disable search and scroll in diff mode (or implement them in `DiffViewer`), and drop the reload. **Fixed 2026-09-25, awaiting a look by eye**: In diff mode the find box and arrows are disabled (`CodeSearchAvailable`) with the count blank, `ScrollToCurrentMatchAsync` does nothing, and pending finding scrolls are held (`PendingScrollsCanLand`) until the single view returns - dropped if the user moves to another class first. `ToggleAnnotations` re-shows the single view with `keepDiff: true` instead of reloading the diff. Held by `CodeReviewDiffModeTests`; `code-review.md` describes it. To check: in diff mode the find box is greyed; click a finding with a line, switch to Single, and it scrolls there; toggling annotations in diff mode shows no spinner. **Checked by eye 2026-09-27, complete**: in diff mode the find box is greyed, a finding clicked there scrolls to its line on switching to Single, and toggling annotations shows no spinner. |

### Analysis, resolution and caches

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B347 ✅ | **Changing a package's imports does not re-analyse the classes below it that live in their own files** | Analysis | ⭐⭐ | M | Since B292 `TypeResolver` reads every enclosing scope's imports, serving dependency analysis, `MissingUnits` and `DeclarationOrder`'s simple-type lookup. Every incremental path - the VCS pipeline, `RefreshDependenciesAsync` (B290), the re-check after a reload - covers only the classes in the changed file. Adding or removing `import Modelica.Units.SI;` in `P/package.mo`, by hand or by a pull, leaves `P`'s children with stale edges and findings until a full re-analysis. Widen the affected set to descendants when a package's `ClassImports` changed; test with a child in a separate file. **Done 2026-09-25**: The new `ModelicaGraph/EnclosingImportChanges.cs` records, before a reload, the imports of each reloaded class that encloses a class in another file, and afterwards adds those descendants to the affected set wherever the imports differ. Both reload paths - `ReloadFileAsync` (Code Review, MCP edits) and `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` (the VCS pipeline, Refresh) - use it, so every caller's refresh and re-check reaches the children; an edit that leaves the imports alone widens nothing. Held by `EnclosingImportReloadTests`. |
| B348 ✅ | **A list import is resolved as a wildcard, so B292's "cannot disagree" does not hold** | Analysis | ⭐ | S | `TypeResolver` treats `A.B.{X}` as `A.B.*`, so `import SI.{Voltage};` makes `Current` resolve to `SI.Current`, while `ReferenceResolver.AddImport` expands the list exactly - rules and dependency edges disagree, though both claim they cannot, and an enclosing package's list import can capture a name that belongs further out. `EnclosingImportTests` covers only the positive case. Also `ModelAnalyzer.VisitImport_clause` collects imports in visit order at every depth, so a nested class's imports leak outward and a reference before its import misses it. Match the listed names; add a negative test. **Done 2026-09-25**: `TypeResolver.ResolveViaImport` resolves only the listed names (and names below them), and `ModelAnalyzer` reads the outermost class's own imports up front with `ReferenceResolver.CollectClassImports` instead of collecting them in visit order at every depth. Held by six new `EnclosingImportTests`, including `AListImport_DoesNotMakeAnUnlistedNameVisible`, `AnEnclosingListImport_DoesNotCaptureANameThatBelongsFurtherOut`, `AnImportWrittenBelowTheReference_StillApplies` and `ANestedClassesImport_DoesNotLeakIntoTheEnclosingClass`. |
| B349 ✅ | **A class's cached icon is invalidated only by its own code, though it depends on its base classes and other libraries** | Library Browser / icons | ⭐⭐ | M | B258 caches `IconRendered`, reset only by the class's own `ModelicaCode` setter, but the render resolves base classes and `modelica://` bitmaps from other classes and libraries. A class extending `Modelica.Icons.*` rendered before MSL loads (a repository added mid-session) is "no icon" for good; editing or pulling a base class's icon never updates its heirs until restart; a render that threw is cached as no icon, and `ParsedCode != null ? Render(ParsedCode…)` is B291's double read, so a concurrent release makes it throw. Before B258 each refresh healed these. Reset on library changes and for heirs of a reloaded class; do not set the flag on a throw; read `ParsedCode` once. **Done 2026-09-25**: `LibraryDataService` counts every tree-changing event (a library added, removed or cleared, a file reloaded) and stamps each icon with the count; an icon with an older stamp is rendered again, only for classes a tree actually shows - which covers every heir of a changed class, not only direct ones. A render that throws leaves the class unflagged, and `ParsedCode` is read once. Held by `IconCacheInvalidationTests` (`AnHeirRenderedBeforeItsBaseLoaded_GetsItsIconOnceTheBaseArrives`, `EditingABaseClassesIcon_ReachesItsHeirs`, and `AskingAgainWithNothingChanged_KeepsTheRenderedIcon` so B258's saving holds); no way was found to force a render to throw, so that path is untested. |
| B350 ✅ | **A class moved to another file is marked as a simulation change** | Library Browser / change markers | ⭐⭐ | M | `ModelChangeClassifier` compares each file with its own committed version, and `VcsWorkingCopyFile` carries no old path, so a renamed file or a class moved by Split into files, Format All or `git mv` finds nothing at HEAD and every class is `Added`, which `ChangeMarker` shows as the simulation marker and the Cosmetic filter hides - the wrong answer for exactly the restructuring MLQT performs. Look for the class id in the committed version of the other changed or deleted files, and carry `OldPath` for renames. Also: `IModelChangeClassifier.Invalidate` has no production caller, so its cache grows across project switches. **Done 2026-09-25**: A class that reads as Added is looked up by name in the committed versions of the other changed and deleted files and compared with that - a class split out of a package comes back Cosmetic, having lost a level of indentation. `VcsWorkingCopyFile.OldPath` is new (Git fills it for staged renames) and a renamed file is compared with its old path; committed versions are parsed once per revision; the classifier clears its caches on `OnProjectChanged`. Held by five new `ModelChangeClassifierTests`, the control `AClassNoCommittedFileHad_IsStillAdded`, and `GitRenameStatusTests`. |
| B351 ✅ | **`UnusedMembersAnalyzer` relies on dependency edges it does not declare it needs** | Analysis | ⭐ | S | Since B281 `ExtendedAmong` uses `UsedByModelIds` whenever `DependenciesAnalyzed`, but `NeedsDependencyAnalysis` is false, so in the GUI it reads incrementally-maintained edges that can be stale (B347) and reports a class's protected members unused because an `extends` edge is missing; it also enumerates a plain `HashSet` that `AnalyzeDependenciesForModelsAsync` may be mutating. Declare the need or state the limit, and snapshot the set under the graph lock. **Done 2026-09-25**: A stress test reproduced "Collection was modified" within milliseconds; `AddModelUsesModel` also wrote both ends of an edge outside the graph lock. `DirectedGraph.GetUsedByModelIds` copies the set under the lock, `AddModelUsesModel` writes under it, and the analyzer and `GetModelUsedBy` read through the copy. The dependency is stated in the analyzer's remarks rather than declared: without edges it is still correct, only slower, and declaring it would switch the rule off where there are none. Held by `ReadingTheEdges_WhileAnIncrementalAnalysisRewritesThem_DoesNotThrow` and `GetUsedByModelIds_IsACopy_AndEmptyForAnUnknownModel`. |
| B352 ✅ | **A lead for B166: parse errors recorded during the check reach only the next run's report** | Checking | ⭐ | S | `LibraryCheckSession` reads `ParserErrorReporter.ToFindings` before the per-class pass, and parse errors recorded lazily during that pass would appear only from the second run in a session. Unmeasured, and it would give a one-way increase rather than the spread B166 recorded - measure it before crediting it. Also noticed: `EnsureDependenciesAnalyzedAsync` can mark the graph analysed after a library added mid-run was invalidated, and `LibraryDataService` ~501 runs an ungated full `AnalyzeDependenciesAsync`. **Done 2026-09-25 - real, and not B166's cause**: `LibraryCheckSession` now reads the parse findings after the per-class pass. Measured over ~22,000 real classes, parsing each on its own recorded nothing the load had not, so B166 stays open. Both side notes were real and are fixed: `DirectedGraph` counts invalidations and `MarkDependenciesAnalyzed(generation)` refuses once one happened (`EnsureDependenciesAnalyzedAsync` then runs again, up to three times), and `AddLibraryFromZipAsync` invalidates like every other load instead of analysing outside the gate. Held by `ParseFindingTimingTests`, `AnInvalidationDuringAnAnalysis_IsNotOverwrittenByItsCompletion`, `EnsureDependenciesAnalyzedAsync_CoversALibraryLoadedWhileItRan` and `AddLibraryFromZipAsync_LeavesTheAnalysisToTheGate`. |

### Library browser, startup and settings

| # | Item | Area | Value | Effort | What is needed |
|---|------|------|-------|--------|----------------|
| B353 ✅ | **Reordering or removing a repository hands each browser's state - busy flag included - to a different repository** | Library Browser | ⭐⭐ | S | `MainLayout.razor` renders the `LibraryBrowser`s with no `@key`, so after B188's `MoveRepository` Blazor reuses them by position with a new `Repository`, and `OnParametersSetAsync` resets none of `_changeFilter`, `_vcsBusy`, `_isLoading`, the panel's expansion, `_expandedNodeIds` or `_selectedNodes`. Moving a repository during a long Update puts `_vcsBusy` on the wrong one and lets a second operation start on it - what B294's guard was for. A status query in flight across the swap also overwrites the other repository's markers (B299's shape, unfixed in this sibling). Add `@key="repo.Id"` and discard a status result whose repository changed while awaited. **Done 2026-09-25**: The browsers are keyed by repository id, and `CheckForUncommittedChangesAsync` numbers its queries and drops an answer overtaken by a newer query or a different repository. Since B326 the busy state is also global, so a second operation could not in fact start; the state handed to the wrong repository was what remained. Held by `LibraryBrowserRepositorySwapTests` (`AStatusQueryThatReturnsAfterTheRepositoryChanged_IsDiscarded`, `TheRepositoryBrowsersAreKeyedByRepository`). |
| B354 ✅ | **Unticking "Reference only" leaves Commit and Revert disabled and no change markers until something unrelated refreshes** | Library Browser / settings | ⭐⭐ | S | Since B191 the status check returns early for a reference-only repository, and the toggle only sets the flag; the browser re-checks on a repository id change, tree change, file activity or VCS event, and a reference-only repository was never monitored. Commit and Revert reappear disabled; ticking it the other way leaves stale markers. The comment claiming the case is handled holds only by luck. Re-check status when `IsReferenceOnly` changes, and start or stop monitoring with it. **Done 2026-09-25**: `IRepositoryService.SetReferenceOnly` sets the flag, starts or stops monitoring with it, discards the cached status and raises `OnRepositoriesChanged`; the settings toggle calls it, and `LibraryBrowser` re-checks status when its repository's flag changed. Held by `SetReferenceOnly_StartsAndStopsTheWatch_AndSaysTheRepositoriesChanged` and `ReferenceOnlyToggleTests`. |
| B355 ✅ | **An empty or new project still never loads the Reference Libraries at startup** | Startup | ⭐⭐ | S | B280 fixed project switching, but both empty-project startup paths in `MainLayout.RunStartUpAsync` return before `LoadReferenceLibrariesAsync`, and adding a repository afterwards does not load them - B280's symptom on the sibling path, while switching *to* the same empty project does load them. B280 added no test. Load them on those paths (or on the first repository added) and guard both. **Done 2026-09-25**: Both empty-project paths go through `OpenProjectWithNothingToAnalyseAsync`, which loads the reference libraries. `ReferenceLibrariesLoadPolicyTests` reads MainLayout's source and requires every `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync` to be followed by `LoadReferenceLibrariesAsync` before its path returns, and `OnProjectChanged` to load them too - the guard B280 lacked; against the old file it fails at the two early returns. |
| B356 ✅ | **The parser-error ancestor set can be cached stale during a bulk load** | Library Browser | ⭐⭐ | S | `ModelsWithDescendantParserErrors` computes from a `GetAllModels()` snapshot and assigns `_descendantParserErrors` unconditionally, so a library add that clears the cache between the two leaves a set built before the last libraries arrived, which the single end-of-load announcement then serves to every tree - what B258's note says must not happen. It also still climbs ancestors with `LastIndexOf('.')`, which B191 replaced for markers because quoted identifiers contain dots. Guard the assignment with a generation, and use the ancestor chain. **Done 2026-09-25**: `RaiseTreeDataChanged` counts invalidations, and a set is kept only if none happened while it was built; ancestors are walked through `ParentModelName`. Held by `ALibraryLoadedWhileTheSetIsBuilt_IsNotLeftOutOfTheCachedSet` and `AQuotedPackageName_IsNotSplitIntoPackagesThatDoNotExist`. |
| B357 ✅ | **Reloading during startup or a project switch leaves file monitoring and resource analysis off for the session** | Startup | ⭐⭐ | M | After a B270 reload `StartupAlreadyRan` skips startup once any repository is loaded, while the old instance's `RunStartUpAsync` runs on against a disposed component, throws at its next `InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged)` and skips steps 5 and 6 - resource analysis and `StartMonitoringAllRepositories`. Edits on disk then go unnoticed with nothing saying so. | B370 ✅ | **`build-and-test.yml` builds in multi-line `run:` blocks, B362's weakness in another workflow** | CI | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B362. Several steps (~35-63, 171-179, 474-480) build more than one project in one pwsh `run:`, so only the last command's exit code counts. Mostly masked because the `--no-build` test steps then fail on a missing assembly, but a middle project that fails to build while a stale binary exists would not stop the job. One command per step, or check `$LASTEXITCODE` after each; extend `TestRunnerScriptTests` to builds. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: every `dotnet build`, `restore` and `publish` in `build-and-test.yml` is now a step of its own, and the Linux CLI publish is split from its smoke test, which runs only on a publish that succeeded. `TestRunnerScriptTests.EveryDotnetCommandIsAStepOfItsOwn` (renamed from `EveryTestSuiteIsAStepOfItsOwn`) holds every workflow file, found rather than listed, to it for test, build, restore and publish, with a self-check that its pattern still matches. |
| B371 ✅ | **`check-coverage.ps1` under Windows PowerShell 5.1 prints assembly figures that look wrong** | Coverage gate | ⭐ | S | Found doing B363 on a machine with no pwsh: the summary read MLQT.Shared 29.3% and RevisionControl 58.8% while the gate passed. Confirm whether 5.1 and pwsh 7 measure or merge differently, or whether these are simply what reportgenerator prints - and if the script needs pwsh 7, say so and refuse under 5.1. **Not a defect, 2026-09-27**: the same collected reports print the same figures under Windows PowerShell 5.1 and pwsh 7.6.6 (MLQT.Shared 33.2%, RevisionControl 59.8%), and 5.1 runs the script end to end. They are reportgenerator's whole-assembly averages, dominated by classes the baseline already accepts (MainLayout 0.1% of 1,057 lines, CodeReview 14.4% of 1,314, SvnRevisionControlSystem 28.7% of 844), while the gate judges per class. The script does not need pwsh 7. |

Reproduced against collected coverage with svn on PATH (`SvnCli` was listed as droppable, now as kept); `-UpdateBaseline` writes the same ledger as before. `CoverageSuitesTests.TheSvnEntriesUpdateBaselineKeepsAreNotCalledDroppableByTheGate` holds the shared decision. |
| B373 ✅ | **Format All Files may restructure a single-file library that lives in a repository, and delete the original** | Formatting | ⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B306, unverified. `FormattingPipeline.SaveAllLibrariesWithFormattingAsync` skips single-file libraries only when `SourceType == File`, and a repository library is Git or SVN, so one would be saved through `ResolveSaveDirectory` beside the file - possibly as a directory, with the original deleted as an orphan. The trap B306 found for Split. Reproduce first; then decide by path, as B306's `WhyNotSplit` does. **Partly fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a decision**: reproduced - a repository single-file library is expanded into a directory and `MyLib.mo` deleted, leaving `SourcePath` pointing at the deleted file (B306's stale state, now B417), and when any new file failed to write the original was still deleted, losing the classes it held. `FormattingPipeline` now deletes no file of a library it skipped, abandoned or could not write completely, and warns the user; a partial failure may leave classes defined twice, which can be cleaned up where a lost class cannot. Held by `FormattingPipelineTests.AClassWhoseNewFileCannotBeWritten_KeepsTheFileItCameFrom` and `ALibraryWhoseSaveCompletes_StillLosesItsOrphans`. Open: whether Format All should expand a single-file repository library at all (`code-formatting.md` says yes, `CodeReview.WhyNotSplit` refuses the same thing by path), or re-register it as a directory library afterwards. **Decided and closed 2026-09-27**: the user decided that when formatting is applied, Format All does split a single-file repository library into one file per class, as `code-formatting.md`'s "One File Per Class" says. What the split left behind (`SourcePath` naming the deleted file) is fixed by B417, and the deletion safety by this item's earlier commit. Code Review's Split still refuses the same split (B306), only because it does not yet call `RelocateLibraryAsync` - see B429. |
| B374 ✅ | **A partial Format All leaves the graph's code different from the disk** | Formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B303. `ModelicaPackageSaver` overwrites each class's in-memory `ModelicaCode` even when writing its file failed. Split now reloads to cover it; Format All does not. Keep the stored code for classes whose file was not written, or reload them. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `ModelicaPackageSaver` stores the rendered text (and clears `SourceMatchesFile`) only when the file holding the class was written - its own file, or the one it lives inline in - through the new `StoreWrittenCode`. Held by `ModelicaPackageSaverTests.SaveLibraryToDirectoryWithResult_AClassWhoseFileWasNotWritten_KeepsTheCodeOnDisk`, `_AClassInsideAFileThatWasNotWritten_KeepsTheCodeOnDisk` and `_AClassWhoseFileWasWritten_StoresWhatWasWritten`. |

Build it from every child. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `ModelicaPackageSaver.AddChildrenSavedElsewhere` adds, for a package with no stored `package.order`, the children the save was not given (those already in files of their own) after the ones it wrote, by name; a package with a stored order is left to it. An exception between the two leaves the monitor off. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: all three bare `StopMonitoring`/`StartMonitoring` pairs on the Code Review page - Split into files, `SaveAnnotatedFileAsync` (Suppress, formatting exclusion, accepted words) and the spelling correction - now take `MonitorPause.Begin` over `GetRepositoriesSharingWorkingCopy`, so a reload that throws no longer leaves the monitor off. `CodeReviewSplitMonitorTests` holds Split by behaviour through the page harness (both repositories paused and restarted, a restart after a throwing reload, no start of an unwatched neighbour), and `CodeReviewMonitorPausePolicyTests`, modelled on B416's MainLayout guard, holds the page to no direct Stop/StartMonitoring and every pause over the working copy. `file-monitoring.md` updated. |
