B342's agent found that instance helpers which never call `StateHasChanged` can be tested on `new CodeReview()`; a bUnit harness with the services faked would cover the rest. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CodeReviewTestBase` in MLQT.Shared.Tests renders the page with all fifteen services over a real graph and one Git repository, and can hold HEAD reads at a gate so a test decides which load lands first. `CodeReviewPageTests` drives select, diff and select-while-loading; `ALoadOvertakenBySelectingAnotherClassDoesNotLandOnIt` and `AfterALoadWasOvertaken_SingleThenDiffStillShowsTheSelectedClass` hold B344's mid-load half, which had been checked only by hand, and both fail with the still-shown guard in `LoadModelDiffAsync` removed. One production addition, `CodeReview.DiffLoadsFinished`, the only way to wait for a load that renders nothing. `skill-gui-testing.md` describes the harness and its pitfalls. |
| B378 ✅ | **"Extends at top" may reorder a record's constructor arguments, as B304 did** | Formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B304, unchecked. If flattening places inherited fields where the `extends` clause stood, lifting `extends` above a record's own fields changes the order of its constructor's inputs. Check against the specification and Dymola; if so, exempt records as `DeclarationKinds.KeepsSourceOrder` does. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed against the specification - MLS 3.6 section 7.1 places inherited elements at the extends clause, and a record constructor's inputs follow the flattened record (12.6). The renderer lifted a record's extends above its fields whenever "imports first" was on; it now writes a record's (and operator record's) extends clauses in source order among its fields, and `ExtendsClausesAtTop` does not report them, both asking `DeclarationKinds.KeepsSourceOrder`. Held by `DeclarationOrderRenderingTests.ARecordsExtendsClause_StaysWhereItIsAmongItsFields`, `AModelsExtendsClause_IsStillLiftedToTheTop` and `ExtendsFirstTests.ARecordsExtendsClause_AmongItsFields_IsNotReported`. Not compared with Dymola. |
| B379 ✅ | **`PackageCodeTrimmer.OwnsItsLines` accepts two classes on one line, and a package then goes untrimmed** | Checking | ⭐ | S | Found doing B309. The line-based check only asks that the last line end with `;`, which `model A end A; model B end B;` satisfies for both; the ranges overlap, `SourceElision.Of` throws, the exception is swallowed and the whole package stays untrimmed. Use the token-position check `ExciseInlineClasses` now has. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `OwnsItsLines` looked at whole lines and did less than it seemed - its leading-whitespace test compared a line with its own `TrimStart()` and was always true, so it only asked that the child's last line end with `;`. Two classes on one line overlapped, `SourceElision.Of` threw and the package went untrimmed; worse, in `model A end A; constant Real k = 1;` the constant was cut out of the text every rule checks. It now uses the nodes' character offsets: only indentation before the child on its first line, only its terminating `;` and whitespace after it, the range running to that `;`'s line, and offsets that disagree with the line numbers keep the child. Held by `PackageCodeTrimmerTests.ChildrenSharingALine_DoNotStopTheRestOfThePackageBeingTrimmed`, `AChildSharingItsLineWithADeclaration_IsKept_AndTheDeclarationWithIt` and `AChildWhoseSemicolonIsOnTheNextLine_IsExcisedWithIt`. |
| B380 ✅ | **The Code Review exclusion toggle saves settings without recording defaults** | Settings | ⭐ | S | Found doing B310, optional. The toggle calls `SaveRepositorySettingsAsync`, which since B310 writes the change but not the defaults an explicit Apply records; `ApplyRepositorySettingsAsync(repository.Id)` is the call an explicit user change should make. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: re-including a class from Code Review now removes it from `FormattingExcludedModels` through `CodeReview.RemoveExcludedModelEntryAsync`, which calls `ApplyRepositorySettingsAsync`, and only when the class was actually in the list - otherwise nothing is written, since recording the defaults then would be a modified file nobody asked for. Held by `CodeReviewExclusionSaveTests` (2). |
| B381 ✅ | **B223's read-only settings warning now fires in fewer cases** | Settings | ⭐ | S | Found doing B310. Since saves skip unchanged files, a repository whose settings file exists but cannot be written is flagged only at its first changed write; the warning on adding the repository now fires only when there is no file yet. Probe writability on load instead. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: on load, when `.mlqt/settings.json` exists and the repository is not reference-only, `RepositoryService` opens the file for writing and closes it again without writing; if that fails, `IsSettingsReadOnly` is set, so the add-time warning fires again. Held by `AnExistingSettingsFileThatCannotBeWritten_IsFlaggedOnLoad` (which skips itself if a read-only file is still writable, as when running as root) and a control showing the probe changes neither the file's content nor its write time. |
| B382 ✅ | **A rebase left in progress cannot be continued or aborted from MLQT** | Git | ⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B327. `GitRebaseDialog` never detects an existing rebase, so once it closes with conflicts there is no Continue or Abort anywhere in the app; B327 now refuses Commit mid-rebase and says to continue or abort, which the user can only do elsewhere. Detect a rebase in progress and offer both. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a look by eye**: `GetRebaseInProgress` finds a stopped rebase (branch from git's `head-name`, files still in conflict) and `Repository.RebaseInProgress` carries it. The Rebase dialog opens on it instead of starting one - conflict list, Continue once every conflict is resolved (at once if none are left), Abort, which now reloads the libraries since they were loaded from the half-rebased files - holding the monitor off. The header names the rebase, and Rebase stays enabled and is highlighted with More actions instead of Create branch. Held by `GitOperationsTests.GetRebaseInProgress_*`, `RebaseInProgressTests`, `GitRebaseResumeTests` and `LibraryBrowserDetachedHeadTests.ARebaseInProgress_*`. To check: stop a rebase on a conflict outside MLQT, open MLQT - the header says the rebase is in progress, Rebase is highlighted and opens on the conflict list; resolve with Edit Externally and Mark as Resolved (see B418), then Continue. Also try Abort. **Checked by a journey 2026-09-27, closed**: `RebaseInProgressJourney` performs the hand check in a real browser - git stops a rebase on a conflict outside MLQT, the header names it, Rebase is highlighted in More actions and opens on the conflict list, Edit Externally and Mark as Resolved enable Continue, and the rebase finishes (or Abort puts the branch back at its old tip), checked on disk and on screen. Fails with the dialog ignoring the rebase in progress, with Rebase disabled on a detached HEAD, or with `RebaseInProgress` never populated. |
| B383 ✅ | **A slow, quiet `svn update` may be killed by the idle timeout** | SVN | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B330. `UpdateRevisionInPlace` runs `svn update --quiet`, and B297's timeout detects a stall by watching for output, so an update of a large working copy that prints nothing for the idle limit is stopped and, since B330, cleaned up. Drop `--quiet` or measure progress another way. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `UpdateRevisionInPlace` no longer runs `svn update --quiet`, so svn reports each file as it goes and the B297 idle limit sees it working; it was the only quiet svn call. An update with nothing to fetch is still silent through the local crawl, measured at about 3 s on 30k files. `SvnCommandRunnerTests.NoSvnCommandIsMadeQuiet` reads RevisionControl's source for any svn command made quiet, so the next one fails too. |
| B384 ✅ | **Refresh reloads every change against the first repository's root** | VCS pipeline | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B325. `RefreshLibrariesAsync` passes the first repository's `VcsRootPath` to `UpdateChangedFilesAsync` even when the pending changes span several working copies, so paths in the others resolve wrongly. Group by working copy. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first - a changed file in a second working copy was stored with the `FileNode` path `A\..\B\Lib\package.mo`, which no path comparison matches. `GraphBuilder.UpdateGraphForChangedFiles` now resolves every changed file to its full path (`ResolveChangedFile`), so the root Refresh passes only anchors the relative paths and no grouping is needed. Held by `GraphBuilderTests.UpdateGraphForChangedFiles_AFileOutsideTheRoot_IsStoredUnderItsOwnFullPath` and `LibraryDataServiceTests.UpdateChangedFilesAsync_AFileInAnotherWorkingCopy_KeepsItsOwnPath`. |
| B385 ✅ | **Format All is not counted as VCS work, so a VCS operation can start during it** | VCS pipeline | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B326. `OnRepositorySettingsApplied`'s formatting does not go through `BeginVcsWork`/`VcsPipelineQueue`, so an Update or Switch can start while Format All is rewriting files - B326's hazard by another route. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a check by hand**: the full reformat goes through the new `VcsPipelineQueue.TryEnqueue`, counted as VCS work from the event and queued behind any analysis pipeline, so no VCS operation or Refresh can start during it; it is refused while any VCS work runs, because an Update in flight holds no place in the queue. On refusal the user is told, the new settings are kept (the files are not reformatted - press Format All Files afterwards) and the findings are still re-checked. Held by `VcsPipelineQueueTests.WorkThatMustNotStartUnderAVcsOperation_IsRefusedWhileOneRuns` and `WorkQueuedWhenNothingRuns_IsVcsWork_UntilItHasFinished`; the MainLayout wiring has no harness. To check: start Format All Files, and the Library Browser's VCS buttons and Refresh stay disabled until it finishes. **Checked by a journey 2026-09-27, closed**: `FormatAllIsVcsWorkJourney` starts Format All Files from the Edit Repository dialog, holds it part-way at the test host's `GatedFormattingPipeline`, and finds Update, Commit, Revert, Switch branch, Create branch, More actions and Refresh all disabled until it finishes and enabled afterwards. Fails with the full format run outside `TryEnqueue`. |
| B386 ✅ | **The SVN changed-files lookup for a revision is unpegged** | SVN | ⭐ | S | Found doing B328, unconfirmed. `svn log <wc url> -r N -v` pegs at HEAD, so it may fail for a revision from before the working copy's branch existed - B328's shape. Peg it with the same helper. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: not the shape suspected - unpegged, a revision from before the branch existed is found through copy history, and pegging at N (B328's fix) would break exactly that (`E160013`). What failed was a working copy whose branch has since been deleted or renamed: nothing is at its URL at HEAD, so every revision came back empty. The URL is now pegged at the later of the working copy's revision and N (`PegForChangedFiles`). Held by `SvnIntegrationTests.GetChangedFiles_FindsARevisionOfABranchDeletedSince_AndOneFromBeforeIt` and three `SubversionContentUrlTests`. |
| B387 ✅ | **Adding or removing a class in a package does not re-analyse its children in other files** | Analysis | ⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B347. A new `P.Voltage` in `package.mo` should capture `Voltage` in `P/Child.mo`, but B347 widens the affected set only when a package's imports change, so this waits for a full re-analysis. Widen on a change to the set of classes a package declares, too. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `EnclosingImportChanges` also records the classes in the reloaded files, and for each that appeared or disappeared adds the classes in other files below its parent whose own text names it as a whole identifier - covering a class added to `package.mo` and a new or deleted file of its own, on both `ReloadFileAsync` and `UpdateChangedFilesAsync`. Narrowed by the name rather than widened to the whole subtree, which would re-analyse a package each time a file is added to it. A class that only inherits a component of the captured type may keep a stale cached resolution; its base, which names the type, is re-analysed. Held by `EnclosingClassReloadTests` (6, including the identifier-boundary cases). |
| B388 ✅ | **A lazily recorded parse error on a nested class reports line 1** | Checking | ⭐ | S | Found doing B352. `EnsureParsed` records errors relative to the class, and `ParserErrorReporter.ToFindings` treats every line as a file line. No load from disk reaches it today; map through the class's location when one does. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `ParserError` now says which frame its line is in (`LineIsClassRelative`), `EnsureParsed` marks what it records, and `ParserErrorReporter.ToFindings` subtracts the class start only from a file line. Held by `ParserErrorReporterTests.AnErrorRecordedByParsingTheClassOnItsOwn_KeepsItsLineInTheClass` and `AClassRelativeErrorAndAFileLineError_AreEachReportedWithinTheClass`, and `ModelDefinitionTests.EnsureParsed_MarksTheErrorsItRecords_AsCountedFromTheClass`. |
| B389 ✅ | **Replacing a class's code in memory keeps its old parse errors and records no new ones** | Checking | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B352. The `ModelDefinition.ModelicaCode` setter clears neither `ParserErrors` nor `MayRecordParserErrors`, so code replaced in memory - as Format All does - reports the old errors and never the new. Reset both in the setter. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the `ModelicaCode` setter empties `ParserErrors` and sets `MayRecordParserErrors` again. `PackageCodeTrimmer` puts the load's diagnosis and the bar back, since trimming only cuts lines and a lifted bar would let a package record a copy of an inner class's error; `SourceSnapshot` now carries both. Held by `ModelDefinitionTests.ReplacingTheCode_*`, `ParseFindingTimingTests.ACheckAfterTheCodeWasReplacedInMemory_ReportsTheNewCodesErrorsNotTheOld`, `PackageCodeTrimmerTests.TrimmingKeeps*` and `SourceSnapshotTests.TheLoadsParseErrorsAreRestored_TheyCannotBeWorkedOutAgain`. |
| B390 ✅ | **The GUI's parse-error surfacing may read before the check parses** | Checking | ⭐ | S | Found doing B352, unexamined. `SurfaceParserErrors` in MainLayout and `StyleCheckingService` may have the ordering B352 fixed in `LibraryCheckSession`. Check, and move the read after the pass if so. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed - the GUI read parse errors only before its check, so errors the check itself recorded reached the Findings list only when something unrelated re-read. The read-and-replace is now `ParserErrorReporter.Refresh`, shared by MainLayout and `StyleCheckingService`; the service runs it when a run completes, and MainLayout again after the deferred dependency pass (that one call has no test, MainLayout having no render harness). | B391 ✅ | **A Bitmap with an `origin` or `rotation` may be placed and turned in the flipped frame** | Icons | ⭐ | S | Found doing B320, unverified. `GetBitmapTransformAttribute` emits `scale(1,-1) translate(origin) rotate(r)`, so both act after the flip, and `y = min(y1, y2)` in that frame looks questionable. Test against a Dymola render and apply B320's ordering if wrong. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed, and wider than suspected - with the image written at y = min(y1, y2) inside the counter-flip, every Bitmap was mirrored about y = 0, so any asymmetric extent was misplaced (MSL's EngineV6_analytic picture at y = -39..75 was drawn at -75..39), and origin/rotation acted in the flipped frame (origin's y negated, rotation clockwise). The top edge is now -max(y1, y2) and origin/rotation come before the counter-flip, B320's ordering. Checked on EngineV6_analytic's icon rendered with its PNG. Held by three `IconSvgRendererTests` that map the image's corners through its transform list. |

MSL is unchanged in extent; Buildings has 52 icons now drawn in their bases' stated extent (e.g. `DHC.ETS.Combined.HeatPumpHeatExchanger`, `DHC.ETS.BaseClasses.CollectorDistributor`) and 9 diagrams whose first base states nothing now use the default. | B395 | **Dymola cannot be browsed to or detected on Linux** | External tools / Linux | ⭐ | S | Found doing B338. `BrowseForDymolaFolder` writes `bin64/dymola.exe` and detection looks only in Program Files; a Linux install is `/opt/dymola-2025x-x86_64/bin64/dymola`. B338's treatment for Dymola. **Also, found doing B338 on 2026-09-27**: `DymolaSettings` detects only in its constructor, before the saved values are applied, so a saved blank `DymolaPath` overwrites what it found and clearing the field turns detection off for good. Fix it as B338 did for OpenModelica: implement `IJsonOnDeserialized` to look again when the loaded path is blank, and add an **Auto-detect** button beside the path that runs the search on demand and blanks the field when nothing is found. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a check on a real Linux machine**: as B338 did for omc. On Linux Browse picks the program or its launcher itself (`IFilePickerService.PickExecutableAsync`, opening in `/usr/local/bin`); on Windows it still picks the installation folder and takes `bin64\dymola.exe`, now also accepting `bin64` itself (`DymolaSettings.DymolaUnder`). `DymolaSettings.CandidateDymolaPaths`/`FindInstalledDymola` look on Windows in the Program Files folders then `dymola.exe` on PATH; on Linux at `/usr/local/bin/dymola`, then the rpm's versioned launchers `/usr/local/bin/dymola-<version>-x86_64`, then `/opt/dymola-<version>-x86_64/bin64/dymola` (newest first, with or without the first hyphen), then `dymola` on PATH - launchers first because Dassault's installation guide says the launcher sets the environment the program needs. `DymolaSettings` implements `IJsonOnDeserialized` so a saved blank path is looked for again, and an **Auto-detect** button beside the path blanks the field when nothing is found. Held by `DymolaInstallDetectionTests` and `SettingsExternalToolsTests`. The Linux locations come from Dassault's Dymola 2021 installation guide (section 1.2) and published setup guides, not a real install, and `external-tools.md` says so. To check on Linux with Dymola: clear the path, save, restart - it comes back as the launcher or `/opt/.../bin64/dymola`; Auto-detect over a wrong path replaces it; Browse opens in `/usr/local/bin` and choosing the launcher fills the field with no warning; a Dymola check from Code Review starts Dymola, gets answers, and Dymola is closed when MLQT stops it (see B411). |

| B402 ✅ | **Hidden annotations and nested classes flash visible during a large class's first paint** | Code Review | ⭐ | S | Found doing B342. B185's lexer-first paint cannot hide anything, since hiding needs the tree. Skip the first paint when something would be hidden, or accept the flash and say so. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a check by hand**: the page's first paint goes through `CodeReview.FirstPaint`, which returns null - no paint, spinner until the parse - when the class has something to hide: a nested class stored in its source (asked of the graph, for a package) or, with annotations hidden, an annotation token (asked of the lexer). A model with annotations shown, B185's case, still paints at once. `CodeReviewLargeClassTests` holds the decision; the harness cannot catch a first paint, since a 64 KB parse wins the race. To check: open a `package.mo` over 64 KB holding nested classes (e.g. `Modelica.Units.SI`) and confirm the nested classes never appear in full before collapsing; hide annotations and open a model over 64 KB and confirm no annotation flashes; show annotations and confirm that model still paints before its colouring arrives. **Checked by a journey 2026-09-27, closed**: `LargeClassFirstPaintJourney` watches every paint with a MutationObserver (the lexer colours `Real` as an identifier, the parse as a type) - a 230 KB package with nested classes is painted once, by the parse, already collapsed; a 200 KB model with annotations hidden shows none; with annotations shown it is still painted by the lexer first. Fails with `FirstPaint`'s early returns removed. On a much faster machine the parse could win that last race, which would fail the third check rather than pass it vacuously. |

Clear the new ones and decide whether the older ones in the Dymola suite are worth clearing. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a clean Release build of the solution now reports 0 warnings (56 before, all in test code - 2 CS8604, 10 xUnit1031, 40 xUnit1051, 4 xUnit2008 - the older Dymola/OpenModelica ones included). Blocking waits are awaited, calls are given `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken`, regex checks use `Assert.Matches`/`DoesNotMatch`, and a nullable field is captured before its lambda; no test's assertions changed. |
| B409 ✅ | **A comment between a class's name and its description string is a parse error, and detaches every class after it from its library** | Parser | ⭐⭐⭐ | M | Found checking B344 by hand, 2026-09-27. Modelica allows a comment anywhere, but the grammar keeps `//` comments as tokens for the formatter and accepts them only where it lists them - `long_class_specifier` is `IDENT string_comment composition`, so `function f` / `// note` / `"description"` fails with *mismatched input ... expecting 'end'*. Error recovery then loses the nesting: in a single-file library every class after the error came back without its enclosing package (`Dissipation9.HeatTransfer...` instead of `BigLib.Dissipation9...`), a reload reported 7,055 affected classes for a 3,853-class library, and those classes left their place in the tree. One comment typed in the wrong place does that to a whole file. Accept `c_comment*` wherever a comment can legally stand, starting with the class header, and have the renderer keep it there. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `string_comment` accepts comments before its STRING and `comment` accepts them before its annotation, only when the STRING or annotation follows, so the choice is taken once and B235's rescan cannot return (an ambiguous variant measured 41 s for 2,000 comments). One change covers every description - a class's (both forms), a component's, a short class's, an enumeration literal's and an equation's. The renderer keeps each comment where it stood, and the MCP description setters replace from the first STRING so they no longer delete it. MSL and Buildings (8,367 files) render byte-identical before and after, the whole-library round trip passes on both, and parse time is unchanged. Held by `DescriptionCommentTests` (parse, `ExtractModels` parents, render, growth) and `DocumentationToolsTests.SetDescriptions_KeepCommentsBeforeTheDescription`. Positions still refused are B430-B432. |
| B410 ✅ | **A class the HEAD side cannot find is diffed against the whole file, which can take the UI down** | Code Review | ⭐⭐ | S | Found checking B344 by hand, 2026-09-27. When `CodeReview.HeadSideOf` finds neither the full name nor an unambiguous short name it returns the entire HEAD file, meant as "the user still sees what HEAD had". For a 7.5 MB, 157,852-line file against a ten-line class that passes `DiffViewer`'s LCS limit (about 1.9M cells of 50M), so every HEAD line becomes a removed row and side-by-side rendered them all; the page showed "Comparing..." and then Blazor's *An unhandled error has occurred*, with nothing in the log. Say the class could not be found at HEAD instead of substituting the file, and give `DiffViewer` a limit on rows as well as cells. Reached there through B409's detached ids, but any class renamed or moved within a large file with no unambiguous short name gets the same path. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CodeReview.HeadSideOf` returns null when the class is in the HEAD file under neither its full name nor an unambiguous short name, or the file does not parse, and the page says the class could not be found at HEAD instead of diffing it against the whole file; a failed HEAD read is reported the same way instead of being diffed as if the error text were the class. `DiffViewer` also refuses to lay out more than `MaxRows` (20,000) rows and says so, keeping the added/removed counts. Held by `CodeReviewHeadSideMissingTests` (through the B377 harness), `DiffViewerRowLimitTests` and three `HeadSideOf` cases in `CodeReviewDiffSourceTests`. |
| B411 | **Stopping a Dymola started through a Linux launcher script may leave Dymola running** | External tools / Linux | ⭐ | S | Found doing B395, unconfirmed. On Linux detection now prefers a launcher shell script (`/usr/local/bin/dymola...`), and `DymolaInterface` starts `DymolaPath` with `Process.Start`, later calling `_dymolaProcess.Kill()` and reading `HasExited`. If the script runs Dymola as a child rather than `exec`-ing it, MLQT holds the shell: `Kill()` ends only the shell and leaves Dymola running, and `HasExited`/`OwnsProcess` describe the shell. Check during B395's Linux check; if real, use `Kill(entireProcessTree: true)`, or set the environment and start `bin64/dymola` directly. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a check by hand**: whether the launcher really runs Dymola as a child was not confirmed (no Linux machine with Dymola), so the change is the one that is right either way: `StopDymolaProcessAsync` and `Dispose` now end the whole process tree MLQT started (`Kill(entireProcessTree: true)`, through `DymolaInterface.KillStartedTree`), where `Kill()` ended only a launcher shell. Held by `MLQT.Services.Tests/DymolaLauncherStopTests`, whose fake launcher (.cmd on Windows, sh elsewhere) starts a child without exec, and which fails against a plain `Kill()`; its Linux branch first runs on the `linux-tests` job. Not covered: a launcher that starts Dymola in the background and exits (B427). To check on Linux with Dymola: set the path to the launcher, run a Dymola check from Code Review so MLQT starts Dymola, run `ps -ef --forest` to see whether the launcher shell is Dymola's parent, then close MLQT and confirm no `dymola` process is left and the port is free. |
| B412 ✅ | **The Code Review parse-error banner gives a different line from the Findings panel** | Code Review | ⭐ | S | Found doing B388. `CodeReview.razor` (around line 157) builds "first at line N" from the raw `ParserError.Line`, which for an error the load recorded in a class nested in a `package.mo` is the file line, while the Findings panel shows the class-relative line through `ParserErrorReporter`. They agree only for top-level classes and for errors a class recorded itself. Take the banner's line from `ParserErrorReporter.ToFindings`. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the banner text is `CodeReview.ParserErrorBannerText`, which takes its count and first line from `ParserErrorReporter.ToFindings`, the same source as the Findings row. `CodeReviewParserBannerTests` renders the page over a class nested in a `package.mo` whose load-recorded error is on file line 7, and asserts the banner says line 2, as the row does. |
| B413 ✅ | **A trimmed package's load-recorded parse error may point at the wrong line** | Checking | ⭐ | S | Found doing B388, unconfirmed. `ToFindings` turns a file-line error into a line relative to the untrimmed class, while every other finding on a trimmed package is in the trimmed stored-source frame, which `ClassLocation.FileLine` (and `CodeReview.razor.cs` around line 1241) maps through `TrimElision.ToSourceLine`. For an error below an excised inline child the excised lines would be counted twice. Likely fix: map file-frame errors through `TrimElision.ToDisplayLine` in `ToFindings`. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed - a load error below a 4-line inline child mapped to file line 12 instead of 8. `ToFindings` now maps a file-frame error into the trimmed text through `TrimElision.ToDisplayLine`, so `ClassLocation.FileLine` and the Code Review page each put the cut lines back once; an error on cut lines goes to the last kept line above the cut. Held by `ParserErrorReporterTests.ALoadErrorBelowATrimmedInlineChild_IsReportedOnItsOwnFileLine` and `ALoadErrorOnLinesTheTrimCutOut_IsReportedWhereTheyWere`. |
| B414 ✅ | **Format All has no parse-error guard** | Formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B389, a risk rather than a demonstrated defect. The incremental formatter refuses a file with syntax errors; `ModelicaPackageSaver`'s full save does not. Since B389 each class it re-renders may record its own errors again, and if an enclosing package's rendered text still contains an inner class's broken text both would record the same error - the duplication `MayRecordParserErrors` exists to prevent. Decide whether Format All should skip files with parse errors, as the incremental formatter does. **Fixed 2026-09-27, awaiting a look by eye**, as the user decided: Format All Files leaves any file with syntax errors exactly as it is and lists it. The rule is the incremental formatter's, asked of the same parse (`ModelicaPackageSaver.SyntaxErrorsInFile`, shared with `RenderFileSource`, on the file's text on disk with its owner's within clause). `FormattingPipeline` checks every file of a library before saving and passes the classes in bad files to the saver as untouched - not parsed, rendered, written or moved, while classes below them in their own files are still written in place - and the bad file, and a bad `package.mo`'s `package.order`, are protected from the orphan sweep, so a single-file library with a parse error is not split, deleted or relocated (B373/B417). `SaveAllLibrariesWithFormattingAsync` returns the skipped files and MainLayout shows them in a warning that stays until dismissed. Format All now parses each file once more, in parallel. Held by four `FormattingPipelineTests` (single-file library, one bad file among good ones, a bad `package.mo` keeping its inline classes, order and children, and nothing skipped) and `FormattingPipelineReportTests`. To check: Format All over a library with one broken file - the warning names it, and the file is unchanged on disk. **Checked by a journey 2026-09-27, closed**: `FormatAllSkipsBrokenFilesJourney` runs Format All Files from the UI over a library with one broken, badly laid-out file - the warning names `Broken.mo` and is still up 8 s later, the file is byte-identical, and a badly laid-out file that parses was rewritten. Fails with `FilesWithSyntaxErrors` returning nothing or the warning dismissing itself. |
| B415 ✅ | **Format All deleted a sibling library's files whose path extends this library's name** | Formatting | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B373. `SaveAllLibrariesWithFormattingAsync` collected a library's original files with `FilePath.StartsWith(library.SourcePath)`, so `.../Lib` claimed `.../LibExtra/package.mo`; when LibExtra was not part of the save (another repository sharing the working copy, a reference library, or a different repository filter) its files were not written and so were deleted as orphans. **Fixed 2026-09-27** in B373's commit: containment is now `PathContainment.IsWithin`. Held by `FormattingPipelineTests.ASiblingLibraryWhoseNameExtendsThisOnes_IsNotTakenForAnOrphan`. |

Settled by B373's decision if the expansion goes; a separate fix (re-register as a directory library, updating `SourcePath` and `RelativePathInRepository`) if it stays. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: after a save that wrote every file, a library whose root class went to a `package.mo` is re-registered through the new `IRepositoryService.RelocateLibraryAsync`, which updates `SourcePath` (through `ILibraryDataService.RelocateLibrary`, under its lock; a library opened on its own as File becomes Directory), `RelativePathInRepository`, the repository's `DiscoveredLibraries` key (what Refresh loads and the saved project's `LibraryPaths`), and saves the project. | B418 ✅ | **In a rebase, "Accept Incoming" fails and "Keep Mine" keeps the wrong side** | Git | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B382. `GitRevisionControlSystem.ResolveConflict` checks out `MERGE_HEAD` for Accept Incoming, and there is none during a rebase, so the choice errors with "Could not resolve"; Keep Mine checks out `HEAD`, which mid-rebase is the branch being rebased onto, not the user's commit being replayed - so it throws the user's change away. Only Edit Externally then Mark as Resolved is right in the rebase dialog. `GetConflictVersions`' ours/theirs in the conflict diff are probably swapped the same way. Needs a decision on what "Mine" means in a rebase; the user's own branch (git's "theirs" during a rebase) is the natural reading. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: decided with the user - "Mine" is the user's own branch in a rebase as in a merge. During a rebase `GitRevisionControlSystem.ResolveConflict` takes Keep Mine from index stage 3 (the replayed commit) and Accept Incoming from stage 2 (HEAD, the branch rebased onto), written through the checkout filters (`TakeConflictSide`, which also handles a side that deleted the file), instead of checking out HEAD/MERGE_HEAD; `GetConflictVersions` swaps its pair so the conflict diff's "Ours" is the user's side. Merges and labels unchanged. Held by the `*_InARebase_*` tests in `GitOperationsTests` on real stopped rebases (including Continue after Keep Mine and a modify/delete conflict), with `ResolveConflict_InAMerge_KeepsMineAsTheCurrentBranch` as the merge control. |
| B419 ✅ | **The SVN history log is unpegged, so a working copy on a deleted branch shows no history** | SVN | ⭐ | S | Found doing B386, not reproduced separately. `GetLogEntries` runs `svn log <wc url> -v -l N`, pegged at HEAD, so once the branch is deleted or renamed on the server the History dialog comes back empty - the shape B386 fixed in `GetChangedFiles`, confirmed there with the svn CLI. Peg at the working copy's revision the same way (`PegForChangedFiles`). **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed with the svn 1.14 CLI that a deleted or renamed branch's URL fails both unpegged and pegged with `-r HEAD:1`, and answers at `url@<wc revision>`. `GetLogEntries` still asks unpegged first (that is what shows commits newer than the working copy), and when svn fails reads the log again at `HistoryPeg`, the working copy's own revision - so for a deleted branch history runs up to the working copy's revision. A single-revision lookup is pegged with `PegForChangedFiles`, as `GetChangedFiles` is. Held by `SvnIntegrationTests.GetLogEntries_ReadsTheHistoryOfABranchDeletedSince_AndShowsNewerCommitsWhileItLives` and two `SubversionContentUrlTests`; `svn-operations.md` says so. |
| B420 ✅ | **A base mapped into a region by `IconMap`/`DiagramMap(extent=...)` is drawn unmapped, and `primitivesVisible=false` is ignored** | Icons / MCP diagrams | ⭐ | S | Found doing B394. MLS 3.6 section 18.6.3: a non-null `IconMap`/`DiagramMap` extent maps the base's coordinate system into that region (keeping the aspect ratio when the base has `preserveAspectRatio=true`), and `primitivesVisible=false` hides the base's graphics while keeping its components and connections. MLQT honours a mapped base only when choosing the coordinate system; it still draws the base's graphics in the derived class's coordinates, unscaled, and draws them even when `primitivesVisible=false`. MSL has no non-null map extent but 4 `IconMap(primitivesVisible=false)` clauses, whose base graphics Dymola would hide. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `IconExtractor` records each extends clause's map as an `ExtendsMap` (region, `PrimitivesVisible`), and the new `GraphicsMapping.Into` rewrites a base's primitives into the region - origin mapped, local geometry scaled, a mirror turning rotations and arcs the other way, a quarter turn swapping the scale's axes, an even scale centred where the base preserves its aspect ratio. Both the icon merge and the MCP diagram (`DiagramImage`, now walking the extends clauses one by one) apply `primitivesVisible=false` and the region. In MSL the base graphics of `Sensors.RelativeAngles` and RobotR3 `AxisType1` are now hidden; in Buildings `HeatRecoveryChiller`'s base icon is flipped and scaled into `{{-600,600},{600,-600}}`. Held by `ExtendsMapTests` (including a property test over rotations and mirrors) and McpServer `DiagramMapTests`; Stryker leaves 5 of 88 mutants in `GraphicsMapping`, judged equivalent. A mapped base's components and connections are not mapped yet (B436). |

A vendor library checked out under a maintained repository's `LocalPath` and updated outside MLQT could be reformatted and written with the outer repository's settings - B84's promise that reference-only is never written, broken by the path B404 found. The formatter should skip files owned by a reference-only repository, asked per file or per class through `GetOwningLibrary`. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed on both incremental paths - `FormatChangedFilesAsync` (Refresh) and `FormatModifiedFilesAsync` (startup, through the outer repository's VCS status) rewrote a nested vendor library's file with the outer repository's settings. `IncrementalFormatter.SelectFilesToFormat` now asks per class whether the owning library (`GetOwningLibrary`) is reference-only, through the new `ReferenceOnlyScope.OwnedByReference`, and `FormattingPipeline` passes that predicate on both paths. Held by `FormattingPipelineTests.AChangedFileOwnedByAReferenceLibrary_IsNotWritten_WhateverSettingsItIsHandedWith` (repository and library flag), `AModifiedFileOfANestedReferenceLibrary_IsNotFormattedWithTheOuterRepository` and a control. |

Check whether either closes it, and if so route the close through `CancelChanges`. **Not a defect, 2026-09-27**: the dialog's options leave `BackdropClick` and `CloseOnEscapeKey` unset, so both fall back to the provider it is shown through - MainLayout's `<MudDialogProvider BackdropClick="false" CloseOnEscapeKey="false">`, the only one the Settings page renders under. | B427 | **A Linux launcher that exits while Dymola runs makes a live session read as gone** | External tools / Linux | ⭐ | S | Found doing B411, unconfirmed - depends on how a real launcher behaves, which B411's Linux check will show. If a launcher backgrounds Dymola and exits, `_dymolaProcess.HasExited` is true while Dymola still answers; `IsAliveAsync` and `GetSessionStateAsync` return false/`Gone` on that without probing, so `DymolaInterfaceFactory` drops the session and starts a second Dymola on the same port, and the first is never stopped (it belongs to no process tree MLQT can reach once the launcher has gone). Fix by probing before reporting Gone when the handle has exited, or by starting `bin64/dymola` directly with the launcher's environment. |

B306's shape - decide by path, not source type. Once Format All has split the library (B373/B417) it is a directory and this no longer applies. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed by failing tests first. The root is now decided by path, in one place: `LoadedLibrary.IsSingleFile` (SourceType File, or a path ending in `.mo` that is not an existing directory) and `LoadedLibrary.RootDirectory`, which `LibraryDataService.GetLibraryInfos`, `ResolveImageFileName` and `FindingExport.LibraryRootsByModel` all ask; `CodeReview.WhyNotSplit` (B306), which already asked by path, uses the same property. Held by `SingleFileLibraryRootTests`: root per source type, a directory named `X.mo`, `GetLibraryInfos`, icon bitmap resolution, and finding export for a single-file library whose source type is Git. |
| B429 ✅ | **Code Review's Split still refuses a single-file repository library that Format All now splits** | Code Review / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B417. `CodeReview.WhyNotSplit` refuses this split (B306) because it left `SourcePath` naming the deleted file; B417 fixed that for Format All through `IRepositoryService.RelocateLibraryAsync`, but `SplitPackageForFinding` does not call it and reloads against `library.SourcePath` (giving the relative path "." for the deleted file). To lift the refusal: when the split package is the library's root and the file was removed, call `RelocateLibraryAsync(library.Id, <dir of the new package.mo>)` and reload against a directory root. Given the B373 decision (formatting splits a single-file library), probably wanted - confirm. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: lifted the B306 refusal, as the user decided. When the split package is the library's root and the split deleted the library's own file, `CodeReview.SplitPackageForFinding` re-registers the library as the new `MyLib/` directory through `IRepositoryService.RelocateLibraryAsync` (`CodeReview.LibraryExpandedBySplit`) before the reload, as Format All does (B417), so the reload resolves against the directory. `WhyNotSplit` keeps only the archive refusal. Held by `CodeReviewSplitSingleFileLibraryTests` (the rendered page over the real services and a Git working copy: split, then a new file's classes are placed in the library, then a fresh project load gives the same library) and the `LibraryExpandedBySplit` cases in `CodeReviewSplitPackageTests`. `code-review.md` updated. |
| B430 ✅ | **A comment after the class at file level, or after `within`, is a parse error** | Parser / save | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B409. `end M; // trailing` at the end of a file fails, and so does `within P; // note`. The grammar change is cheap and unambiguous (`c_comment*` after `within ... ;` and after each top-level `class_definition ';'`), but those comments sit outside every class's source span, so a per-class save would probably drop them silently where today the parse error at least surfaces them. Check the save path, and how the existing comments before `within` survive it, before accepting them. **Investigated 2026-09-27, blocked on B445**: no grammar change made. Every save path that rebuilds a file from a class's stored source (Format All, Split, `RenderFileOwnerModel` for spelling fixes and the MCP format tool, the MCP edit tools through `WithinClause.Ensure`) drops all text outside the class - including a comment before `within`, which parses today. Format All already drops a comment after `within` or after the last `end` despite the parse error; only the incremental formatter and FormattingRevert, which render the file from disk, keep them. Accepting these positions would remove the only visible sign while the text keeps being dropped, so the file-level text has to be carried through those paths first (B445); then the grammar change is cheap. **Fixed 2026-09-27**, unblocked by B445: `stored_definition` accepts comments after the within clause and after the file's last top-level class, the trailing run inside the group that starts with a class so every choice is one token ahead (with it outside, a licence header was rescanned per comment - 16x the lookahead for 4x the comments). A comment between two top-level classes is still refused: nothing carries it and Format All writes each class to its own file, so accepting it would turn a visible error into a silent deletion. `PreprocessCode` no longer appends its `;` inside a trailing comment - it made every save write `// trailer;`. Format All and the incremental formatter write the same file for both positions. MSL and Buildings (8,367 files) are byte-identical in the save render, viewer render and highlighter, the fidelity round trip passes on both, parse time unchanged. Held by `FileLevelCommentTests` (growth counted by ANTLR's profiler rather than timed), a classifier corpus entry and `FileHeaderTests.FormatAll_WritesCommentsAfterTheClauseAndAfterTheClassExactlyAsTheIncrementalFormatterDoes`. |
| B431 ✅ | **Comments inside expressions, modifications, function arguments and array literals are parse errors** | Parser | ⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B409. `Real x(start=1, // why` and a table row `[1, 2; // row` both fail - plausible in user code, especially data tables. Accepting them means widening the expression grammar and teaching the renderer to write them back inside expressions, without reintroducing B235's quadratic rescan. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: every bracketed list - modification, extends modification, annotation, call arguments, array, matrix/data table, external call, enumeration - takes comments after each `,` or `;`, after the opening bracket and before the closing one; an enumeration also before a `,`; and a comment before constrainedby inside a modification parses. The closing run belongs to the bracketing rule and is taken only after a list, so every loop ends one token ahead. A comment before a `,` or `;` outside an enumeration stays refused on purpose: taking it made every separator a prediction (+2.15M, +61% over MSL and Buildings, parsing 5-8% slower). The renderer keeps each comment where it stood and continues the list on the next line; lists without comments render as before. MSL and Buildings (8,367 files) render byte-identical in the save render, viewer render and highlighter, the fidelity round trip passes, and parse time is unchanged. Held by `ListCommentTests` (parse, render, stability, viewer, hiding, which rule owns a comment, parents, refusal, lookahead growth via `ParseGrowth`) and a classifier corpus entry. Still refused: comments between an operator and its operand, after if/then, in subscripts and in connect(). |
| B432 ✅ | **Smaller comment positions are still parse errors** | Parser | ⭐ | S | Found doing B409. A comment before a class's top annotation (`// c` then `annotation(...)`, then elements), after the `,` in an enumeration list, before `;` in an equation, before `constrainedby`, and between `extends X(...)` and its annotation. Each needs its own grammar and renderer change. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: all five listed positions now parse, on B409's terms - a comment belongs to the new position only when the token it needs follows (annotation, literal, semicolon, constrainedby), so the choice is made once. The renderer keeps each comment where it stood, with two exceptions: a comment before a semicolon is written after it on its own line, as a comment after an equation already is; and a comment before a class's leading annotation moves to the end of the class with that annotation, where the renderer has always put it, and is hidden with it. MSL and Buildings (8,367 files) render byte-identical in the save render, the viewer render and the highlighter; the whole-library round trip passes; parse time is unchanged. Held by `CommentPositionTests` (parse, parents, render, stability, hiding, `BehaviorExtractor`, growth) and a classifier corpus entry. Still refused: a comment before a comma or the closing parenthesis of an enumeration, and before constrainedby inside a modification (B431's territory). |
| B433 ✅ | **In a merge, Keep Mine and Accept Incoming may fail on a modify/delete conflict** | Git | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B418, by reading. The merge path of `ResolveConflict` still checks out `HEAD`/`MERGE_HEAD` for the path, which fails when that side deleted the file, so only Edit Externally would work there. The rebase path's `TakeConflictSide` (B418) already handles a deleted side and could serve both. Confirm with a real merge first. **Not a defect, 2026-09-27**: confirmed with real modify/delete merges through `MergeBranch` in both directions - the merge path's forced `CheckoutPaths` of HEAD or MERGE_HEAD deletes the working file when that side has no such path, and `Commands.Stage` records the removal, so both choices resolve to the chosen side, deletion included. A test now holds it, `ResolveConflict_InAMerge_WhereOneSideDeletedTheFile_TakesTheChosenSide`, asserting the expected missing stage first; making Keep Mine check out MERGE_HEAD fails it. Whether Commit Merged Changes then commits a staged deletion correctly was not traced. |
| B434 ✅ | **The older merge-conflict tests pass without testing anything when no conflict arises** | Testing / Git | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B418. `ResolveConflict_KeepMine_WithConflictedFile_Succeeds`, `..._AcceptIncoming_...`, `..._MarkResolved_...` and `GetConflictVersions_WithMergeConflict_ReturnsBothVersions` `return` early if LibGit2Sharp's merge produces no conflict, and assert only `Success`, never which content was kept. Make the setup certain to conflict (fail rather than return if it does not) and assert the content, as B418's merge control does. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the four tests now share `MergeStoppedOnAConflict` in `GitOperationsTests`, which fails the test if the LibGit2Sharp merge does not stop on the file instead of returning early. Each asserts which content was kept: `GetConflictVersions` returns main/feature exactly, and `AssertResolvedAs` checks that the file on disk and the staged blob hold the kept side and no conflict remains. Renamed to say what they hold (`..._KeepsTheCurrentBranch`, `..._TakesTheMergedBranch`, `..._StagesTheEditedFileAsItIs`). Checked by mutation: swapping HEAD and MERGE_HEAD fails Keep Mine and Accept Incoming, dropping the stage fails Mark Resolved, and a setup that does not conflict fails all four. |
| B435 ✅ | **A project switch that fails before `OnProjectChanged` leaves its progress published for the rest of the session** | Startup | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B423. If `SwitchProjectAsync` throws before raising `OnProjectChanged` (e.g. `SaveRepositorySettingsAsync` fails) or returns early because the project is not found, `NavState.StartupStep` stays "Loading libraries from repositories" - and MainLayout's own six-step dialog already stayed open, `_startupProcessRunning` left true. Since B422/B423 a reload afterwards also shows the non-closable earlier-run dialog for ever, and B422's guard skips startup. The three callers in `SettingsRepositories` (`ConfirmProjectName`, `LoadProject`, `DeleteProject`) do not catch the failure; a shared helper there that raises `ProjectSwitchStarting`, awaits the switch and on failure clears the step and closes the dialog would cover all three. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `ConfirmProjectName`, `LoadProject` and `DeleteProject` now switch through `SettingsRepositories.SwitchToProjectAsync`, which listens for `OnProjectChanged` during the switch; when it does not come (the switch threw, or the project was not found) it calls the new `AppState.ProjectSwitchAbandoned`, which clears `StartupStep` and raises `OnProjectSwitchAbandoned` - on which MainLayout closes its six-step dialog - and shows an error snackbar saying what failed. Held by `SettingsRepositoriesProjectSwitchTests` (a throw and a not-found switch each clear the step and report the error, a successful switch is not abandoned, all three callers use the helper, MainLayout closes the dialog). The service is still left with no project open after a not-found switch (B440). |
| B436 ✅ | **The components and connections of a base mapped by `IconMap`/`DiagramMap(extent=...)` are not mapped** | Icons / MCP diagrams | ⭐ | S | Found doing B420, which maps a mapped base's graphics. What the base contributes as components is still placed in the derived class's coordinates as the base wrote them: the connectors drawn on an icon (`DiagramComponent.Children` from `ConnectorsOn`) and the inherited components and connect lines on an MCP diagram. The one real case is Buildings `Templates.Plants.HeatPumps.Components.HeatRecoveryChiller`, whose comment says the map puts `port_*2` at the top, and MLQT still draws those ports where the base put them. No library in the corpus uses a non-null `DiagramMap` extent. `skill-icon-extraction.md` records it as not done. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CoordinateMap` (ModelicaParser/Icons) is now the one map for everything a mapped base contributes - primitives (`GraphicsMapping.Into` uses it), placements and connect-line points. `DiagramGeometry.BaseMaps` composes it down the extends chain per layer and `Placements` maps every inherited placement with it, so the connectors on an icon (`ConnectorsOn`), the diagram's components and the router's line ends (`PortOf`) all follow, and the image maps a base's own Line points too. `HeatRecoveryChiller`'s `port_*2` are now on the upper half of its icon, and on `Validation.HeatRecoveryChiller` `PortOf` gives (+-60,40) and (+-60,-32), exactly where that model's Dymola-drawn lines end. Held by `CoordinateMapTests` (a mapped placement draws every icon point where the map puts it, over rotations, mirrors and off-centre origins) and `DiagramMapTests` (icon connector flipped to the top, a line ends on it, a DiagramMap maps components and lines, maps compose). |

Adding them needs debt entries with reasons - "needs a live omc" and "nobody has written the tests yet" being different facts, as the ledger requires - and a clean re-record of the baseline. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: both assemblies are in `$MlqtBars` at 80%, `check-coverage.ps1` runs their suites with the `Requires!=` filters, and the code-coverage job builds them. | B439 ✅ | **Nothing holds the build's warning count at zero** | Build | ⭐ | S | Found 2026-09-27: B408 brought a clean Release build to 0 warnings, and within hours tests written in parallel had added 13 more of the same kinds, fixed by hand. NuGet's vulnerability audit (NU1901-NU1904) stays a warning, being news about a dependency rather than a change anybody made. Checked by planting a `#warning` in MLQT.Cli: a `CI=true` build failed with CS1030 as an error, a plain build succeeded with it as a warning. | B440 ✅ | **A switch to a project that is not found leaves the service with no project open** | Projects | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B435. `RepositoryService.SwitchProjectAsync` saves settings, clears every repository and the graph, and sets `_activeProjectId` to the requested id before checking the project exists; when it does not, it returns with nothing loaded and the active id naming no project. Since B435 the UI closes its progress and says what failed, but the previous project is not restored, and whether the bad id is saved at the next settings save is unverified. Check existence first, before anything is cleared. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `SwitchProjectAsync` now looks the project up before it saves, clears or changes anything, and for a missing id returns having done none of those and without raising `OnProjectChanged`, which `SwitchToProjectAsync` (B435) reads as not switched. Confirmed before the fix that the missing id was also written as `ActiveProjectId` at the next save. `RepositoryServiceTests.SwitchProjectAsync_ToAProjectThatDoesNotExist_LeavesThePreviousProjectOpen` holds it: previous project still active, its repository and graph still loaded, and the save still names it. Create, rename and delete were checked and do not repeat the shape; delete has a different one (B442). |
| B441 ✅ | **Format All may delete a class stored in its own file that the layout says must be inline** | Formatting | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B414, by reading, not reproduced. In `ModelicaPackageSaver.WriteModelFiles`, if a child class is stored in its own file but `PackageFileLayout.StandaloneChildNames` does not list it as standalone (its name colliding with a sibling's or a reserved entry, say), the saver calls `UpdateNestedChildren`, mapping the child into the parent's `package.mo` - but the parent's rendered source does not contain it, since it was never inline. The child's own file then looks orphaned and would be deleted, and the class lost. Reproduce first; the fix must never let a class's only copy go. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: reproduced first - `foo` inline beside `Foo.mo`, or `package sub` inline beside `Sub/`, and Format All deleted `Foo.mo` or the whole `Sub/` directory, losing the class. A child already stored in a file of its own is now written back there whatever the layout says, and a nested class is mapped only to a file written from the file it came from. Last line: `SaveResult.UnplacedModelIds` lists every class the save put in no file, and Format All then deletes nothing of that library and reports it failed. Held by `FormattingPipelineTests` (`AClassInItsOwnFile_WhoseNameDiffersFromAnInlineSiblingsOnlyInCase_IsKept`, `APackageInItsOwnDirectory_...`, `ALibraryTheSaveCannotPlaceEveryClassOf_LosesNoFile`), and two Linux-only cases (`Package.mo`, and `Foo.mo` beside `FOO.mo`) that skip on Windows and first run in CI's `linux-tests`. The `replaceable`/`redeclare`/`inner`/`outer` route cannot reach this: such a file does not parse, and B414 leaves it alone. |
| B442 ✅ | **Deleting the active project saves a dangling active id, and could leave it saved** | Projects | ⭐ | S | Found doing B440, unconfirmed. `RepositoryService.DeleteProject` removes the active project and starts an unawaited `SaveRepositorySettingsAsync` while `_activeProjectId` still names it and its repositories are still loaded; `SettingsRepositories.DeleteProject` then switches to the first remaining project, whose switch saves twice. The fire-and-forget save may take its snapshot before the switch and finish writing after it, putting back the deleted project's id and a stale project list; and if the follow-up switch throws, the service is left with an active id naming no project. Startup's fallback to the first project hides both on the next launch. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first - holding the unawaited save from `DeleteProject(active)` until the follow-up switch had saved got the deleted id back as `ActiveProjectId`, and a follow-up switch that threw left `GetActiveProject()` null. The panel has only ever offered Delete for an inactive project (`getting-started.md` says so), so its delete-then-switch branch was unreachable and is removed; `IRepositoryService.DeleteProjectAsync` now refuses the active project, awaits its save, and puts the project back if the save fails, and the panel awaits it and reports a failure. Held by `RepositoryServiceTests.DeleteProjectAsync_TheActiveProject_IsRefused_SoNoSavedActiveIdNamesADeletedProject`, `_ReturnsOnlyOnceItsSaveHasLanded` and `_WhenTheSaveFails_KeepsTheProjectAndThrows`, and `SettingsRepositoriesProjectSwitchTests`. Create and rename still save without waiting (B447). |
| B443 ✅ | **Format All cannot save a directory whose `package.mo` holds a non-package class** | Formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B441. If `Lib/package.mo` declares `model Lib` rather than `package Lib` with `Lib/Child.mo` beside it, the loader accepts it and `Lib.Child` is in the library, but the saver writes `Lib` as a file (`WrittenAsDirectory` needs `ClassType == "package"`) and has nowhere to put `Child`. Before B441 that deleted `Lib/package.mo` and `Child.mo`; B441's guard now deletes nothing and reports the library failed, but a new `Lib.mo` is written beside the untouched directory, so `Lib` is defined twice until cleaned up. MLS section 13.4.2 has `package.mo` hold the package's definition, so this is not a legal layout: refuse such a library before writing anything (and report it as a load-time finding), rather than writing half of it. **Fixed 2026-09-27, and the row's premise corrected**: MLS 3.6 section 13.4.1 asks only that `package.mo` "define a class A", and forbids only a short class definition as the enclosing class of sub-entities - so `model Lib` in `Lib/package.mo` with children beside it is legal, and no load-time finding was added. Format All now refuses it before writing anything: `ModelicaPackageSaver.NonPackageDirectories` finds a class heading a `package.mo` that the layout would write as a file (a non-package or a short class definition), the saver returns with nothing written (`SaveResult.NonPackageDirectoryIds`), and the pipeline keeps the library and tells the user the class is not a package - no stray `Lib.mo`, nothing deleted. B441's `UnplacedModelIds` guard is unchanged and now also asserted at saver level. Held by `FormattingPipelineTests` (`ALibraryTheSaveCannotPlaceEveryClassOf_LosesNoFile`, `ADirectoryWhosePackageMoIsAShortClass_IsLeftAsItIs`, `AModelInlineInPackageMo_NamedAfterTheDirectory_IsNotTakenForTheDirectory`). Supporting the layout instead of refusing it is B448. |
| B444 ✅ | **The incremental formatter leaves a class's line mapping stale while `SourceMatchesFile` stays true** | Formatting / findings | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B400, by reading, not reproduced. `IncrementalFormatter.FormatAndWriteAsync` stores each class's re-rendered code but does not touch `StartLine`, `StartIndex`/`StopIndex`, `TrimElision` or `SourceMatchesFile`, and the VCS pipeline does not reload the file afterwards. For a class nested in a `package.mo`, a format that changes the line count above it would make `ClassLocation` map findings to the old position; for a trimmed package the elision describes the pre-format file. `ClassSource` is safe (B343's exact check falls back to the stored text); line mapping in reports - Findings, SARIF, the CLI - is the exposure. Reproduce first; the fix is probably the one B374 made for Format All (store what was written, and say the source no longer matches the load). **Fixed 2026-09-27**: reproduced first - a finding in the second class of a `package.mo` whose first class the format lengthened was mapped to line 4 where the written file has it at line 10, and a trimmed package through its pre-format elision; the classes also kept re-rendered code when the write failed. `IncrementalFormatter` no longer re-renders each class's stored code: after each successful write it takes the file's classes back out of the text written, as a load would (code, `StartLine`/`StopLine`, `StartIndex`/`StopIndex`, `SourceMatchesFile` true, elision cleared), trims again any package that had been trimmed, and stores nothing for a file whose write failed (B374's rule on this path). B374's approach - clearing `SourceMatchesFile` - would not do here, since `StartLine` is itself stale below a lengthened class. `IncrementalFormatterWriteTests` holds the nested class, the trimmed package (including `ClassSource.For`), agreement with a fresh load, and the failed write. |
| B445 ✅ | **Format All, Split, spelling fixes and MCP edits delete a file's header comment** | Save / MCP | ⭐⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B430. Any comment above `within` - a licence or copyright header, which ExternData has - is outside every class's source span, and `SaveLibraryToDirectoryWithResult`, `RenderFileOwnerModel` and the MCP writers (`EditTools`, `ClassBodyEditor`, `DocumentationTools` through `WithinClause.Ensure(ownerCode, ...)`) rebuild the file from the class's `ModelicaCode`, so the header is gone after one Format All or one MCP description edit; the same paths drop a comment after `within` or after the last `end`. The incremental formatter keeps all of it (it renders from disk). Reproduce with `// header` / `within Lib;` / `model M ... end M;` under Format All, or `SetClassDescription` on a package whose `package.mo` starts with a comment. Fix: carry the file-level text on the file-owner node and write it back on every whole-file path, with a test per path; decide where it goes when a class is moved, renamed or split into a directory. Blocks B430. **Fixed 2026-09-27**, as the user decided (the header stays with the class that heads the file): the text outside a file's classes - a header above `within`, a comment after the clause or after the last `end` - is read once at load by `FileLevelText` onto `ModelNode.FileText` of the class heading the file, never into `ModelicaCode`, and put back by `WithinClause.Ensure`/`Set` on every whole-file writer: Format All and Split (the rendered paths write it as the renderer does, so they match the incremental formatter), `RenderFileOwnerModel`, and the MCP edit tools (exactly as written). A rename keeps it, a move takes it along (above the class when moved into another class's file), and a split puts it on the new `package.mo` and gives the new per-class files none. Reproduced first on every path (12 tests failing). Held by `FileHeaderTests` in MLQT.Services.Tests and MLQT.McpServer.Tests, `FileLevelTextTests` and `FileTextLoadTests`. Not carried: comments between two top-level classes in one file. |
| B446 ✅ | **A leading class annotation and an external clause swap annotations on save** | Parser / renderer | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B432, confirmed with a render, pre-existing. `ModelicaRenderer.VisitComposition`/`WriteComposition` gives the external clause `context.annotation()[0]`, but when the class also has a leading annotation that is index 0: `function f annotation(Inline=true); input Real x; external "C" g(x) annotation(Library="lib"); end f;` renders `external "C" g(x) annotation (Inline=true);` and moves `Library="lib"` to the class annotation - a formatter changing what a function links against. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CompositionAnnotations` (ModelicaParser/Helpers) sorts a class body's leading, external-clause and trailing annotations by position, and every reader that indexed `composition.annotation()` or read them all as the class's now asks it - the renderer, `ExternalResourceExtractor`, `ModelAnalyzer`, `MlqtSuppressionWriter`, `ModelExtractorVisitor`, the MCP `set_class_documentation` (which wrote Documentation into the external clause), `ClassBodyLocator` (which inserted new elements inside the external clause), the token classifier, `CheckClassAnnotations`, `MlqtSuppressionExtractor`, `DocumentationExtractor` and `ExperimentAnnotationChecker`. An external clause with no annotation of its own also took the class's; fixed too. An Icon on an external clause no longer counts as the class's icon (`CheckClassAnnotationsTests.ExternalAnnotation_IconIsNotTheClasses`). MSL and Buildings render and extract identically (neither has either shape, over 168 external clauses). Also makes B432's leading-comment lookup null-safe, clearing the build's last warning. Held by `CorrectingCodeOrder`'s B446 cases, `CompositionAnnotationsTests`, `ModelAnalyzerExternalAnnotationTests` and two `DocumentationToolsTests`. |
| B447 ✅ | **Creating and renaming a project save without waiting, so a slow save can land after a later one** | Projects | ⭐ | S | Found doing B442, unconfirmed. `RepositoryService.CreateProject` and `RenameProject`, and one more `_ = SaveRepositorySettingsAsync()` near line 1381 of `RepositoryService.cs`, start a save they do not await; each captures its contents when called, so a slow write can land after a later save and put back an older project list or active id (e.g. a rename landing after a following switch's save restores the old active id). Await the save and make these Task-returning, as B442 did for delete; `HeldSettings` in `RepositoryServiceTests` can confirm it. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first by holding the create's (and a rename's) save until a following switch had saved - each landed last and wrote the previous active id back; a stale `ClearAllRepositories` save could also put a deleted project back. `CreateProject`/`RenameProject` are now `CreateProjectAsync`/`RenameProjectAsync`, await their save and undo the change if it fails; the panel awaits them and reports a failure. `RemoveRepository` and `ClearAllRepositories` keep their unawaited save (synchronous callers, effects a failed save cannot undo), so `SaveRepositorySettingsAsync` is now serialised and reads its contents once it is its turn - the last save to land is always the newest - and their failures are logged. Held by the B447 region of `RepositoryServiceTests` (`HeldSettings.HeldWriteLanded`) and four new tests in `SettingsRepositoriesProjectSwitchTests`. Two writes still bypass the gate (B452). |

That touches `PackageFileLayout.WrittenAsDirectory` and needs a decision on whether a non-package class's inline children get split out into files. **Decided and closed 2026-09-27**: the user decided the clean refusal B443 added is enough - it leaves the library untouched and says which class to declare as a package.
