| B450 ✅ | **The `package.order` a Split writes for a package with none is sometimes in a different order** | Formatting / package split | ⭐⭐ | S | Found 2026-09-27: B375's test `PackageSplitterTests.WithNoPackageOrder_TheNewOneNamesTheChildrenAlreadyInTheirOwnFiles` failed about one run in three on the same build, writing `Beta` (already in its own file) before `Alpha` (the class the split moved). The first guess was wrong: `ModelicaPackageSaver` precomputes a package's element names only when `Definition.ParsedCode` is still held, but making it always `Borrow` the tree made both that test and a variant fail every time, and releasing the tree in the test did not reproduce the failure without it. So something else decides the order - where `AddChildrenSavedElsewhere` starts a list the precomputed names should have started, or what the parse of the stored (trimmed?) package source yields. Find the real cause; the written order must not vary between runs. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the saver was not the cause. `GraphBuilder.LoadModelicaFile` set a package's `NestedChildrenOrder` from every file naming it as parent, so `Sub/Beta.mo` wrote `["Beta"]` over the `["Alpha"]` read from `Sub/package.mo` whenever the parallel directory load finished it last, and `BuildPackageOrderList` puts that order first (9 failures in 20 runs, confirmed by instrumentation). It is now set only on a package the file itself defines, which also removes the same run-to-run variation in the library tree's child order for a directory package with no `package.order`. Held by `GraphBuilderTests.LoadModelicaFile_AClassInItsOwnFile_LeavesItsPackagesSourceOrderAlone` and `PackageSplitterTests.WithNoPackageOrder_TheNewOneIsTheSameWhicheverFileLoadedLast`, which force the failing load order; the B375 test passed 40 of 40 runs after the fix. |
| B451 ✅ | **`LibraryBrowserUpdateTests.WhileThePipelineAnUpdateStartedRuns_NoOtherOperationCanStart` fails as "canceled" under full-suite load** | Testing / VCS pipeline | ⭐⭐ | S | Found 2026-09-27: it failed three times in full `MLQT.Shared.Tests` runs by different agents (once reported "canceled after 2.5s") and passes every time on its own, including after the component tests' default wait was raised to 10 seconds - so it is not a bUnit wait running out: an `OperationCanceledException` escapes. Reproduce under load (the full suite in a loop, or the class beside CPU-heavy tests) and capture the stack before changing anything; if the cancellation comes from the browser's own update or pipeline path rather than the test, it is a product defect, not a flaky test. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: not a product defect, and no `OperationCanceledException` - the "canceled" is bUnit's `WaitForFailedException` on the final wait. The test's `pipelineStarted` TaskCompletionSource completed synchronously on the pipeline's pool thread, so when the pipeline started after the test reached its await (under full-suite load) the rest of the test ran inline on that thread and its final `WaitForAssertion` blocked the pipeline it was waiting for. Both completion sources now use `RunContinuationsAsynchronously`; a `Task.Delay` before `TrySetResult` reproduced it every time without the fix. 12 consecutive full MLQT.Shared.Tests runs clean afterwards; the trap is written up in `skill-gui-testing.md`. |
| B452 ✅ | **Two settings writes still bypass B447's save serialisation** | Projects | ⭐ | S | Found doing B447. `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync` does its own read-modify-write of the settings key, and the legacy migration in `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync` calls `SetAsync` directly; neither goes through the new save gate. Both run before anything is loaded, so a race is unlikely, but if one overlapped a queued background save the older contents could still land last. Take the same gate around both. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync`'s read-modify-write and `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync`'s read and legacy migration write now take the B447 save gate; the load releases it before adding repositories, since adding one saves. Those were the service's only other writes of the settings key. Held by `RepositoryServiceTests.CreateAndSelectProjectAsync_WaitsForASaveQueuedBeforeIt` and `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync_LegacyMigration_WaitsForASaveQueuedBeforeIt`, both confirmed failing without the fix. |
| B453 ✅ | **`run-mutation.ps1 -All` leaves out the two tool assemblies the coverage gate now measures** | Build scripts | ⭐ | S | Found doing B438, which put DymolaInterface and OpenModelicaInterface under the ratchet. The mutation script runs each suite unfiltered, so including them would start Dymola and omc for every mutant; it needs a way to pass B399's `Requires!=` filter to Stryker's test run, or a documented decision that they stay out. **Decided 2026-09-27: they stay out.** Stryker 5.0.0's `test-case-filter` (config file only) is read by its VSTest runner alone; the MTP runner selects tests by its own test-uid filter (the string appears only in Stryker.Configuration.dll). Measured with `"test-case-filter": "Requires!=Dymola"` in `DymolaInterface.Tests/stryker-config.json` and `-Mutate '**/DymolaSettings.cs'`: Stryker captured coverage for 266 tests (the suite has 267, 240 filtered), coverage timed out test after test, and Dymola.exe started two minutes in. VSTest is no way round it, since xUnit v3 is MTP. Written down in `run-mutation.ps1` and CLAUDE.md; a single run of either project warns that it starts the tool; `LiveToolTestFilterTests.TheMutationCampaignLeavesBothToolAssembliesOut` holds the list until a Stryker release passes the filter to MTP. |
| B454 ✅ | **An unidentified test in MLQT.Services.Tests failed once in four full runs** | Testing | ⭐ | S | Found doing B452: one failure in one full run of four with no code changed, and the console output did not name it. It may have been B375's package.order test, since fixed as B450 - that was failing about half the time on `backlog` then. Run the suite repeatedly with `--report-trx` to see whether anything else still fails, and treat it as B451 was (find the cause, never a retry). **Not reproduced after B450, 2026-09-27**: MLQT.Services.Tests (Release) run 12 times in a row with `--report-trx`, part of the time on a loaded machine; every run 1,682 tests, 0 failed, and no Failed outcome in any of the 12 TRX files. The one failure in four runs was most likely B375's package.order test, which B450 fixed. |
| B455 ✅ | **A save before the first load erases unmigrated legacy repository settings** | Projects | ⭐ | S | Found doing B452, unlikely in practice. If a save runs before the first `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync` on a legacy settings file, it writes only `Projects` and replaces the legacy `Repositories` list, so those entries are lost before the migration can move them. B452's gate keeps file and memory agreeing but does not keep that data. Nothing normally saves before the startup load; refusing a save until the first load has run (or migrating first) would close it. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first, and wider than the row - the same early save replaced every saved project, not only a legacy list, with one empty Default. Until the first `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync`, a save writes the settings key only when nothing is saved there yet (it still writes each repository's `.mlqt/settings.json`), so nothing is replaced before the load reads it; a service that never loads (the MCP server, most tests) still persists over an empty store. Held by `RepositoryServiceTests.ASaveBeforeTheFirstLoad_KeepsUnmigratedLegacyRepositories`, `_KeepsTheSavedProjects` and `_OverAnEmptyStore_StillWrites`; B447/B452's tests unchanged and green. |
| B456 ✅ | **The MCP `rename_class` tool may leave a standalone class in a file of its old name** | MCP / EditTools | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B445, unconfirmed. Renaming `Lib.M` stored in `M.mo` rewrote the declaration inside `M.mo` and, as far as the code shows, left the file name and `package.order` unchanged, so `model Renamed` sits in `M.mo` - breaking Modelica's file-per-class mapping (MLS 13.4), so the class would not be found where a tool looks for it. Confirm, then rename the file (and a directory package's directory) and the `package.order` entry with it, keeping B445's file header. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first (5 MCP tests failing) - renaming `Lib.M` rewrote the declaration inside `M.mo` and left the file name and `package.order` alone. `rename_class` now moves a file named after its class to the new name before writing it, so the write keeps the file's encoding, line endings and B445 header; it reloads the old path so the old ids go, and renames the `package.order` entry, for a class nested in a directory package's `package.mo` too. A rename onto an existing file is refused before anything is written; a file never named after its class, and a library's own file, keep their names. The preview says the file will be renamed. Held by `RenameClassStorageTests` in MLQT.McpServer.Tests. |
| B457 ✅ | **A class body holding only an annotation parses it as a leading one, so an MCP edit adds the first element after it** | Parser / MCP | ⭐ | S | Found doing B446, pre-existing. In `model M` / `annotation(Icon());` / `end M;` the grammar's optional leading `annotation ';'` matches before the empty element list, so `ClassBodyLocator` has no trailing annotation to stop at and puts `BodyEndOffset` at `end`, after the annotation; an MCP edit adding the first element to such a class writes it after the class annotation. Decide in `ClassBodyLocator` (or `CompositionAnnotations`) that an annotation with no element after it is the trailing one. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CompositionAnnotations.Of` reports a leading annotation with nothing after it in the body (no element, section, public/protected or external clause) as the trailing one, so `ClassBodyLocator` stops at it and `add_component`/`add_equation` write before it (`StructureEditToolsTests ...HoldingOnlyItsAnnotation...`, `CompositionAnnotationsTests`). The renderer now finds the comments written with a pre-list annotation by position, not as Leading, or an annotation-only body lost them (`CommentPositionTests`). MSL and Buildings (8,367 files, 331 annotation-only bodies) format, view-render, highlight and extract identically before and after. |
| B458 ✅ | **A file whose `within` clause does not name the directory it is in loads without complaint** | Libraries / checking | ⭐ | S | Found doing B449: `Lib/R.mo` saying `within Lib.Q;` loads as `Lib.Q.R`, though MLS 3.6 section 13.4 has a file in a package's directory declare `within` that package. Format All then cannot place it (B449's guard refuses the library, deleting nothing), and other tools would not find the class where MLQT shows it. Report it where the user will see it - a structural finding beside the existing `MLQT.Structure.*` rules, whose registration guards CLAUDE.md describes - rather than loading it silently. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: new graph analysis `MLQT.Structure.WithinClause` (Structure, Error when enabled) reports a file in a loaded package directory whose within clause does not name that package (MLS 3.6 section 13.4.3: "the within-clause shall designate the class of the enclosing entity"), and a `package.mo` naming the wrong parent; a missing `within` below the top level is reported, top-level entities are not judged, and the expected name is built from the root's loaded name and the nested `package.mo` class names, so one wrong `package.mo` is one finding. Runs through the shared pipeline, so the GUI, `mlqt check` and MCP report alike. `WithinClauseAnalyzerTests` (14) holds the cases, `CrossToolParityTests.AWithinClauseThatDoesNotMatchItsDirectory_IsReportedAlikeByEverySurface` the agreement, `CheckPipelineTests` and `QualityToolsTests` the CLI and MCP. No findings on MSL, ModelicaTest, ModelicaServices, ModelicaReference or Buildings. **Changed 2026-09-27, as the user decided**: the rule is off by default (opt-in like most rules), still at Error when enabled; `RecordDefaults` writes nothing for it. `DefaultOnRuleTests` again names only `SingleFilePackage`, the analyser, parity and CLI tests enable the rule, and `CheckPipelineTests.AWithinClauseThatDoesNotMatchItsDirectory_IsNotReportedByARunWithNoSettings` holds the default. |
| B459 ✅ | **The parser's timed growth tests fail about one full parallel run in three** | Testing / parser | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B430. The growth tests B409 and B432 added (`DescriptionCommentTests`, `CommentPositionTests`, and any like them) time a parse at two sizes and compare; under a full parallel run the timing is noise and they failed about one run in three, though the same parse measures linear on its own - and CI now runs in parallel on shared runners. B430's `FileLevelCommentTests` counts lookahead with ANTLR's profiler instead, which is deterministic and still fails against the quadratic grammar. Move every timed growth test to that. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: every timed parse-growth test (`TrailingCommentParseTests` for B235, `DescriptionCommentTests` for B409, `CommentPositionTests` for B432) now goes through `ParseGrowth.AssertLinear` in ModelicaParser.Tests, which counts the lookahead ANTLR's profiler records instead of timing the parse; B430's `FileLevelCommentTests` uses the same helper. Each fails against the quadratic grammar it was written for (about 16x against a bar of 8x; the grammar as it is measures 4.0x), and ModelicaParser.Tests passed 10 full parallel runs of 2,566 tests with no failure. CODING_GUIDELINES §Testing says to write new growth tests this way. Two ModelicaGraph timing assertions of another kind are B461. |
| B460 ✅ | **Creating a project at startup over a legacy settings file strands the legacy repositories** | Projects | ⭐ | S | Found doing B455, unlikely in practice. On a legacy file (no Projects, a non-empty `Repositories`), `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync` appends a project, so Projects is no longer empty and `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync`'s migration - which runs only when there are no projects - is skipped; the next save writes a collection without `Repositories` and they are gone. Run the migration after every read of the key (a shared `MigrateLegacy(settings)` in `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync`'s read-modify-write as well as the load). **Fixed 2026-09-27**: confirmed first (3 tests failing). One `MigrateLegacyRepositories` helper now runs after both reads that write the key back, `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync` and `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync`, each still under B452's save gate. It migrates whenever a top-level `Repositories` list is non-empty, not only when there are no projects, so a file already stranded by the old path is recovered too; it picks a free "Default"/"Default N" name so it never takes the name the user typed, and clears the list so a second read migrates nothing. Held by `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync_OverALegacyFile_KeepsTheLegacyRepositoriesInAProject`, `CreateAndSelectProjectAsync_NamedDefault_OverALegacyFile_KeepsBoth` and `LoadRepositorySettingsAsync_LegacyRepositoriesBesideProjects_AreMigratedOnce`. MainLayout's `hasLegacyRepos` check is narrower than the migration now, harmlessly: a file with both goes through the silent load, which migrates it. |
| B461 ✅ | **Two ModelicaGraph tests assert on elapsed time** | Testing / graph | ⭐ | S | Found doing B459. `GraphNodeRemovalTests` (around line 180) asserts by Stopwatch that removing nodes all at once is 10x faster than one at a time, and `IntegrationTests` (around line 145) that loading and analysis finish within 5 s - B459's flaky shape, on a CI runner that now builds in parallel and fails on less. Neither has been seen to fail. Count the operations instead (as `ParseGrowth` counts lookahead), or drop the absolute threshold. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `DirectedGraph` counts its passes over every node's edge set (internal `EdgeSetScans`), and `GraphNodeRemovalTests` asserts `RemoveNodes` takes one pass for 50 nodes where a `RemoveNode` loop takes 50 - a `RemoveNodes` reimplemented as that loop fails it (verified by mutation). `IntegrationTests`' 5 s bound had nothing to count and could only fail on a loaded runner, so it is dropped; the renamed `LargeGraph_LoadsAndResolvesAChainOfDependencies` checks every link of its chain instead. CODING_GUIDELINES §A growth test counts work says both. |
| B462 ✅ | **The formatter writes a multi-row matrix or data table on one line, past the maximum line length** | Parser / formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B431. `VisitPrimary`'s `[` branch joins every row with `"; "` and never wraps, so a Buildings `TimeTable(table=[0, 15 + 273.15; 8*3600, 20 + 273.15; ...])` written one row per line comes back as a single line of about 150 characters on save - across MSL and Buildings today, which is why B431's byte-identical check could not show it. B431 keeps row breaks only where a comment forces one. Wrapping tables row by row, or at the line length, would change a lot of existing output, so it needs a decision first: keep the rows as the author wrote them, wrap only past the line length, or always one row per line. **Fixed 2026-09-27**, as the user decided (keep the author's rows): a matrix row that starts a line in the source starts one on save, a level in from the line the matrix began on; rows written together stay together and a one-line table stays on one line, read from the tokens' line numbers as B431's comments are, so a re-render is stable. The lines a matrix ends inside an argument list wrapped for length are moved to the level of its last line, which also fixes B431's misaligned comment rows there. Over MSL and Buildings, 232 of 8,367 save renders change (41 MSL, 191 Buildings) and every change is only line breaks and indentation; in 108 of them text after a table also moves, because the line-length wrap now measures the shorter last row. The longest line in those files drops from 21,272 to 8,788 characters. The highlighter is unchanged, and `ClassChangeClassifier` rates re-laying out a table as Cosmetic. Held by `MatrixRowTests` (21). |
| B463 ✅ | **A matrix's first row written on the line after `[` is pulled up onto the `[` line** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B462, the other half of the user's "keep the author's rows" decision. Buildings often writes `table=[` with the first row on the next line (e.g. `CDL/Integers/Validation/Equal.mo`); the formatter pulls it up. Apply B462's token-line check to the `[` as well, measured over MSL and Buildings as B462 was. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the `[` now gets the same token-line check as each row's `;` (B462), so a first row written below the `[` stays on its own line, a level in like the rows after it; a comment after the `[` already ended that line and is unchanged. Over MSL and Buildings, 86 of 8,367 save renders change (3 MSL, 83 Buildings), every change only line breaks and indentation; all re-parse clean, are stable on re-render, and the highlighter is unchanged. Held by 7 new tests and 4 re-render stability cases in `MatrixRowTests`. |
| B464 ✅ | **An argument list wrapped for length leaves its first argument on the opening line, past the maximum length** | Parser / formatting | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B462, pre-existing. `TimeTable timTabLin(smoothness=Buildings.Controls.OBC.CDL.Types.Smoothness.ConstantSegments,` stays at about 110 characters on the declaration line and only the later arguments wrap, though the source had `(` then a new line; many Buildings validation models are affected. When a list has to wrap, the first argument should start the continuation if keeping it on the opening line breaks the limit - measure what it changes over MSL and Buildings first. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the first argument of a modification, or of a call's named arguments, is written and then moved to a continuation line a level in when it took the opening line past the maximum length - measured on what was rendered, not the source text, so an argument the renderer itself breaks over lines keeps its layout, as do graphics-annotation lists, a list inside one written an argument a line, and a list whose `(` is followed by a comment. Over MSL and Buildings, 1,017 of 8,367 save renders change (167 MSL, 850 Buildings), every change only line breaks and indentation, all re-parse clean and are stable, and the highlighter is unchanged; lines over 100 characters in those files fall from 13,796 to 12,318. Held by `FirstArgumentWrapTests` (21, mutation-checked on every guard and the boundary). |
| B465 ✅ | **A graphics annotation's wrapped arguments are indented less than the line they continue** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B464, pre-existing. In MSL `Blocks/Discrete.mo` (ZeroOrderHold's Icon) `graphics={Line(points=...` is written 10 spaces in and its next argument `color={0, 0, 127}` 8 spaces in, so a continuation is shallower than the line it continues. B464 deliberately left graphics lists alone. Measure over MSL and Buildings before changing, as B462-B464 did. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `EmitLine` indents a line by the level it is ended at, and the last line of a list wrapped for length is ended after the list has gone back out a level, so it could land shallower than the line it continues. A line that starts an argument of a call's wrapped named arguments (B464's moved first argument included) is now ended at least a level in from the line the call's `(` is on (recorded when the `(` is written, since a comment after it ends that line first); it only ever adds spaces, so no wrap decision changes. Over MSL and Buildings, 150 of 8,367 save renders change (21 MSL, 129 Buildings), only in indentation and only to the right; all re-parse clean, are stable on re-render, and the highlighter is unchanged. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests` (8, mutation-checked). |
| B466 ✅ | **A nested modification's wrapped arguments line up with its own name** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B464, pre-existing. A nested modification that is itself on a continuation line writes its wrapped arguments at the same column as its name (MSL `Fluid/Fittings.mo`: `m_flow(` with `min=`/`max=` below at the same indent). B462 fixed the same thing for matrices by indenting from the line's leading spaces; argument lists still indent from the level only. Measure over MSL and Buildings before changing. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: B465's rule now applies to a modification's argument list too (wrapped for length, written an argument a line, or its first argument moved by B464), so a nested modification on a continuation line has its wrapped arguments a level in from its name - MSL `Fluid/Fittings.mo` now writes `m_flow(` with `min=` and `max=` both two spaces further in. Over MSL and Buildings, 94 save renders change beyond B465's (242 of 8,367 for the two together), only in indentation and only to the right; all re-parse clean, are stable, and the highlighter is unchanged. | B467 ✅ | **A wrapped argument whose own list wraps is written a level deeper than its siblings** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B465/B466 - the same emit-time root cause (a line is indented by the level it is ended at), a different shape. When an argument's own list wraps, its line is ended inside the extra `Indent()` and comes out 2 spaces deeper than a sibling that ended normally: at max length 40, `Tt tt(aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa=1, sub(a=1, b=2, c=3));` gives `aaa...=1,` at 4 and `sub(a=1,` at 6; Buildings' DryCoil has `nomVal=` 2 deeper than `spe=`/`perCur=`. The fix moves lines left, so measure over MSL and Buildings first. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a line that starts an argument wrapped for length is ended no deeper than the level its siblings are ended at, so an argument whose own list wraps no longer comes out a level past them (Buildings' DryCoil now has `nomVal=` at the column of `spe=` and `perCur=`); B465/B466's floor still applies after it, and a data table that begins on such a line moves only its rows back (B462), not that line a second time. Three expectations B465/B466 had recorded in this shape move two spaces left. Over MSL and Buildings, 145 of 8,367 save renders change (25 MSL, 120 Buildings), 946 lines each two spaces to the left and nothing else; all re-parse clean, are stable, and the highlighter is unchanged. Held by 2 more `ContinuationIndentTests` and 3 updated ones (mutation-checked). |
| B468 ✅ | **Array elements that are wrapped calls step deeper one after another** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B467. In Buildings' DryCoil and ApparatusDryPoint, `sta={Stage(...), Stage(...), Stage(...), Stage(...)}` keeps each following `Stage(` on the previous element's last line, and B465's floor then puts each Stage's arguments a level deeper than the last (`spe=` at 4, 6, 8, 10); those lines also run to about 170 characters. Array-constructor elements that are long calls are not wrapped onto lines of their own. Measure over MSL and Buildings before changing. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: once an array constructor has already been written over more than one line, a call element that would not fit after the one before it (with a wrapped argument's three-character margin) starts a line of its own, a level in from the line the array opened on - or, inside a list written an argument a line, at the column the element before it ended at. Buildings' DryCoil now has each `Stage(` at one column with `spe=` at one column. Graphics arrays, non-call elements, and an array still on its opening line are left alone (B474). Over MSL and Buildings, 63 of 8,367 save renders change (10 MSL, 53 Buildings), only in line breaks and indentation; all re-parse clean, are stable, the highlighter is unchanged, and lines over 100 characters in those files go from 888 to 779. Held by 5 more `ContinuationIndentTests` (mutation-checked). |
| B469 ✅ | **An argument wrapped for length inside a list written an argument a line gets two extra spaces** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B467. In MSL `ModelicaTest/Rotational.mo` a graphics `Text(...)` has its siblings at 12 and a long multi-line `textString=` at 14: the named-args list wraps every argument because of the graphics level, and the long one also takes the wrap-for-length indent and prefix. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a named-argument list written an argument a line anyway (a graphics element's, or one inside a list written that way) no longer also gives an argument too long for its line a wrapped argument's indent; MSL `ModelicaTest.Rotational`'s `textString=` is now at the column of `extent=` and `textColor=`. Over MSL and Buildings, 4 save renders change (all MSL), 6 lines each two spaces to the left and nothing else; all re-parse clean, are stable, the highlighter unchanged. Held by 1 more `ContinuationIndentTests` test (mutation-checked). |
| B470 ✅ | **An argument's own expression continuation sits at the argument's column** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B467. In MSL `Media/package.mo`, `state := ThermodynamicState(p=p,` is followed by `T=(...)/cp_const` and then `+ reference_T);` at the same column as `T=` - the continuation of an argument's expression is not indented in from the argument. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a line an arithmetic expression wraps onto (before `+` or `-`) is kept a level in from the indent level the expression is written at, rather than the level the line is ended at (after any wrapped argument list has gone back out); MSL Media's `+ reference_T` is now two spaces in from `T=`. The early returns are gone, and the merge test now asserts that merging `feature-test` succeeds and raises `OnRepositoriesChanged`. The commit result's skipped files are B478. |
| B473 ✅ | **Two images in Documentation/Images are neither generated nor on the photographs list** | Docs | ⭐ | S | Found doing B152. `metrics-1.png` is a photograph of MSL the Metrics page links to; `settings-reference-2.png` appears to be referenced by no markdown file. | B475 ✅ | **An equation's right-hand side continued after a wrapped `=` sits at that line's column, not a level in** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B470 - its shape in the `=`-wrap context. Buildings' TwoPortMatrixRLC and Evaporation and MSL PartialFriction write `= productAC1p(...) + ...` at 6 and the `+ ...` continuing it also at 6 (before B470 it was at 4, left of the line it continues). It comes from the level the `=` wrap is written at. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a line an arithmetic expression wraps onto, in an equation's right-hand side written after a wrapped `=`, is kept a level in from the line the right-hand side starts on (the `=` line carries its own continuation indent, so B470's rule gave that line's own column); Buildings' TwoPortMatrixRLC now has the `+` two spaces right of the `=`. Over MSL and Buildings, 10 of 8,367 save renders change (4 MSL, 6 Buildings), 20 lines each two spaces to the right and nothing else; all re-parse clean, are stable, the highlighter unchanged. Held by 1 more `ContinuationIndentTests` test and a stability case (mutation-checked, including the reset after the equation). |
| B476 ✅ | **Graphics elements in a non-class annotation have their arguments at the element's own column** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B468, pre-existing. In a connector's `Diagram(graphics={Polygon(...), Text(...)})` (Buildings `CHPs/BaseClasses/Interfaces/ModeTypeOutput.mo`) `Polygon(lineColor=...` is followed by `fillColor=` at the column of `Polygon(`: the graphics detection (`_classAnnotation` plus `graphics=`) does not fire for an annotation that is not the class's. B468 now puts the following `Text(` at that same column, consistent with `Polygon(` but inheriting the shape. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the annotation in question is a short class definition's (`connector X = output T annotation(...)`), which the class-annotation graphics detection did not see. Any annotation's `graphics=` array inside an Icon or Diagram written an argument a line now takes the class annotation's layout, an element a line with its arguments a level in; an `Icon(graphics={...})` on one line keeps its graphics wrapped for length as before. MSL's `Blocks.Interfaces` RealInput/RealOutput and Buildings' CDL connectors are among them. Over MSL and Buildings, 22 of 8,367 save renders change (7 MSL, 15 Buildings) - exactly the 22 files with the shape, none left - only line breaks and indentation; all re-parse clean, are stable, the highlighter unchanged. Held by 2 more `ContinuationIndentTests` tests and 2 stability cases (mutation-checked). |
| B477 ✅ | **About 16 `RepositoryServiceTests` use fixed MSL/Buildings checkouts, assert nothing without them, and one merges into the real MSL checkout unguarded** | Testing | ⭐⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B471. Tests in `MLQT.Services.Tests/RepositoryServiceTests.cs` (lines ~106-460, and `MergeBranchAsync_WithValidGitRepository_ReturnsResult` near 537) use `@"C:\Projects\ModelicaStandardLibrary"` or `@"C:\Projects\modelica-buildings"` and return early when the folder is missing, so they pass without asserting on CI, on Linux and on any other machine - and the Git merge test runs `MergeBranchAsync` against the developer's real MSL checkout without the `SandboxedId` guard, the hazard that guard exists for. Give them temp Git repositories (`CreateTempGitRepo` / `GitTestRepositoryFixture`), or mark any that genuinely need MSL/Buildings by need (as live-tool tests are), never by returning early. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the unguarded merge test was rewritten before anything ran, so it never touched the real MSL checkout. None of the 15 tests needed a real library, so each now builds its own under the temp directory with LibGit2Sharp (`TempGitLibrary` in `RepositoryServiceTests`: an MSL-shaped repository with several libraries, a nested package and a hidden `.CI` package, or a Buildings-shaped one with a library in a subdirectory). Every repository id goes through `SandboxedId` and every early return is an assertion, several of them stronger than before (the exact set of libraries discovered, the loaded library's `RepositoryId`). The merge test is now `MergeBranchAsync_WithValidGitRepository_MergesTheBranch`, merging a branch the test made and asserting the file arrives and `OnRepositoriesChanged` is raised. Checked by mutation (hidden-directory skip, nested discovery, `RepositoryId`, merge event). Nothing in the repository names `C:\Projects\ModelicaStandardLibrary` or `C:\Projects\modelica-buildings` any more; the same file's other silent skips are B479. |
| B478 ✅ | **An SVN commit's skipped files are reported with backslashes on Windows** | SVN | ⭐ | S | Found doing B472. `SvnRevisionControlSystem` (around line 1436) builds `VcsCommitResult.SkippedFiles` with plain `Path.GetRelativePath`, the shape B472 fixed for working-copy status; it is shown in the commit dialog and passed back to commit. Route it through `VcsRelativePath.Canonical`, and check what it is passed back to still matches. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `SvnRevisionControlSystem.Commit` builds `VcsCommitResult.SkippedFiles` through `VcsRelativePath.Canonical`, so the commit dialog lists `.mlqt/settings.json`, the same string as the working-copy status it reloads for the follow-up commit. `SvnMergeCommitTests.Commit_MergeScenario_SkippedFileCommittedSuccessfullyInSecondCommit` asserts the shape, that each skipped file appears in the reloaded status by the same string, and that passing the list back to `Commit` succeeds. This was the only relative path RevisionControl still handed out with backslashes: the other `GetRelativePath` calls already use `Canonical`, build paths used only inside the method, or already use forward slashes. Merge results' full paths are B480. |
| B479 ✅ | **`RepositoryServiceTests`' temp-repository tests still skip themselves silently, and leak their repositories on Windows** | Testing | ⭐⭐ | S | Found doing B477. Outside B477's tests the same file has about 41 `if (tempDir == null) return;` and about 52 `if (!addResult.Success ...) return;` guards (e.g. around lines 680-1060, 1287-1580 and 2579-2960): `CreateTempGitRepo` shells out to the git CLI and returns null on any failure, so with git missing or broken those tests pass without asserting anything, and a failed add passes a test named for the operation after it; the Local VCS-error tests (around 1287-1575) return early whenever the temp directory is not detected as Local. Build them with LibGit2Sharp (reuse B477's `TempGitLibrary`), let failures throw, and assert `addResult.Success` / `VcsType`. `CreateTempGitRepo`'s cleanup also leaks every repository on Windows (`Directory.Delete` fails on git's read-only object files inside a swallowed catch); `TempGitLibrary.Dispose` clears those attributes first. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `CreateTempGitRepo` and `RunGit` are gone. Every temp-repository test in `RepositoryServiceTests` builds its repository with `TempGitLibrary` (LibGit2Sharp, with new `WithPackage`, `CommitAll`, `WriteAndStage` and `CreateBranch` helpers), which throws on failure and clears read-only attributes on dispose. Every early return is now an assertion (`addResult.Success` with its `ErrorMessage`, `VcsType` Local, log entries, library id), and the one remaining unguarded repository id goes through `SandboxedId`; `ClearAllRepositories_WithGitRepo_RemovesAll` had passed trivially and now checks the add first. None of them was hiding a failure: all 147 tests pass as assertions. Checked by mutation: a throwing helper fails every git test, and a local directory turned into a git repository fails all 17 Local tests. The old helper had left more than 70,000 `RepoServiceTest_` directories in the developer's temp directory; the new tests leave none. The same shape in RevisionControl.Tests is B481. |
| B480 ✅ | **Git and SVN merge results may give their files' full paths in different shapes** | VCS | ⭐ | S | Found doing B478, unchecked. `VcsMergeResult.ConflictedFiles`, `TreeConflictedFiles` and `ModifiedFiles` are full paths from both systems: Git builds them with platform separators, and SVN's merge result passes on whatever path its status parser holds. Check the two agree (a real merge with a conflict through each, on B426's per-run SVN repository and a temp Git repository), and that every consumer (the merge and conflict dialogs, `VcsDialogOutcome`, the pipeline that reloads after a merge) compares them normalised. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a real merge through each system (a conflicted file, a cleanly merged one, and for SVN a file added on both sides) showed `ConflictedFiles` and `TreeConflictedFiles` already agreed, as full `Path.GetFullPath`-form paths, but Git's `ModifiedFiles` was always empty: it read what was staged after a merge that had already committed, and a conflicted merge never set it. Git now builds all three through `VcsRelativePath.ToFullPath` and lists the diff the merge made, or what a conflicted merge staged, and `VcsMergeResult` documents the shape. No consumer compares the paths as strings (and nothing in the app reads `ModifiedFiles` yet); the merge dialogs' conflict rows now show the forward-slashed relative form, like the dirty-file list above them since B472. Held by `GitOperationsTests.MergeBranch_WithAConflict_GivesEveryPathInFullPlatformForm` and `MergeBranch_NonFastForward_ListsTheFilesTheMergeChanged`, `SvnMergeCommitTests.MergeBranch_WithConflicts_GivesEveryPathInFullPlatformForm` and `VcsConflictRulesTests.AFileInTheWorkingCopy_IsShownRelativeToIt_ForwardSlashed`. |
| B481 ✅ | **RevisionControl's Git and SVN integration tests pass silently when their fixture fails or their data is missing** | Testing | ⭐⭐ | M | Found doing B479. `GitTestRepositoryFixture` builds its repository with LibGit2Sharp but catches any exception and sets `RepositoryAvailable = false`, so about 187 tests in `GitIntegrationTests` (21) and `GitIntegrationAdvancedTests` (166) return on `if (!_repositoryAvailable) return;` having asserted nothing; about 35 more in `GitIntegrationAdvancedTests` return on conditions the fixture guarantees (`entries.Count < 2`, `hash == null`, `!File.Exists(readmePath)`, `!renameResult.Success`, `remoteBranch == null`), so a regression that empties a log or fails a rename passes the test named for it. The same shape is in `SvnOperationsTests` (29) and `SvnIntegrationAdvancedTests` (12) inside the svn-needing classes. Let the fixture's exception propagate and turn those returns into assertions, finding out why for any that then fails. `HookCommandTests`' git-CLI guard is deliberate and documented; leave it. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: `GitTestRepositoryFixture` throws when it cannot build its repository, so a broken fixture fails every test using it (checked by mutation: all 219 Git integration tests fail, nothing left in temp). `GitIntegrationTests`, which cloned the GitHub ModelicaEditorTests repository for every test and skipped 21 tests offline, now builds its own copy of the fixture per test. All 187 availability guards are gone, and about 55 returns and conditional asserts on conditions the fixtures guarantee, across `GitIntegrationAdvancedTests`, `SvnIntegrationAdvancedTests` and `SvnOperationsTests`, are assertions - all pass, so none was hiding a failure. `SwitchBranch_ToRemoteBranch_CreatesLocalTrackingBranch` had never run (the fixture has no remote); it now clones the fixture and asserts the tracking branch (mutation-checked). `SvnOperationsTests`, which CI runs without svn, uses `Assert.SkipUnless` there and is reported as skipped rather than passed. Without svn only the three filtered classes fail, so the filter is unchanged. The other SVN working-copy classes are B486. |
| B482 ✅ | **A declaration's description is not counted when deciding whether its last argument fits** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B474, which exposed it: once an array wraps, the last line is short, so the next argument (`final nLay=3)`) joins it and the description string after `)` runs past the limit - FLEXLAB `Construction2.mo` now has `...GypsumBoard(x=0.01588)}, final nLay=3) "South wall in test bed X2"` at 105 characters (16 of B474's 23 new over-100 lines, each shorter than the line it replaced). Count the description when deciding whether the closing argument fits, or put the description on its own line when it does not. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a short class definition's description that would take its line past the limit now starts a line of its own, a level in, through the same check a component's description has always had (`WrapBeforeDescription`); an enumeration's stays after its `)`, which already starts a line. FLEXLAB Construction2 now ends `...final nLay=3)` with the description below. Over MSL and Buildings, 101 files change (25 MSL, 76 Buildings), only in line breaks and indentation; lines over 100 in them go from 2,569 to 2,429, and every new long line is part of a longer one it was split from. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests.AShortClassDescriptionThatDoesNotFitStartsALineOfItsOwn`. |
| B483 ✅ | **An array of calls in a call's positional arguments wraps mid-list** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B474. Positional arguments are never wrapped for length, so only the array breaks: MSL `MultiBody.Joints.Internal.InitAngle` gives `R_rel = Frames.axesRotations(sequence_start, {angle[1], angle[2], angle[3]}, {der(angle[1]),` then `der(angle[2]), der(angle[3])});`; Buildings EnergyPlus `RoomModel.mo`'s `newInput({...}, {pre(TLast), pre(XLast),` has the same shape. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a call's later positional argument can now be moved as a first argument can (B474), so an array of calls in it still on its opening line moves the argument to a line of its own before wrapping, and wraps from there only if it still does not fit. Over MSL and Buildings, 2 files change, exactly InitAngle and EnergyPlus RoomModel, only in line breaks and indentation. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests.AnArrayOfCallsInALaterPositionalArgumentMovesTheArgumentBeforeWrapping`. |
| B484 ✅ | **A wrapped array that is not a first argument cannot be moved off its line** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B474, pre-existing. Buildings' `IEEE_34_AL120.mo` `redeclare ... Generic cables={LowVoltageCables.PvcAl120(),` still starts on a 109-character line: the array is a later argument, so there is no first argument to move, and nothing moves a later argument onto a line of its own before its array wraps. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the row's diagnosis was incomplete - IEEE_34_AL120's argument already starts its line (the list is written an argument a line), so moving a later argument could not help. Now an array with no argument to move, whose line is already past the limit when a call in it has to wrap, moves to a line of its own after its `=` (`MovedArrayPastTheLimit`), only if what it has written then fits and not when a comment after the `{` ended its line. Over MSL and Buildings, 2 files change (IEEE_34_AL120, PartialDamperExponential), only in line breaks and indentation; lines over 100 in them 19 to 17. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests.AnArrayWithNoArgumentToMoveIsMovedOffALineItHasTakenPastTheLimit`. |
| B485 ✅ | **An arithmetic wrap inside an if-expression or an array comprehension is not indented as a continuation** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | S | Found doing B475, pre-existing. MSL `Rotational/Translational.Interfaces.PartialFriction` and `MassWithStopAndFriction` wrap `... else if startBackward then sa` / `+ f0_max/unitForce else if ...` inside an if-expression's branches, and `PolyphaseElectroMagneticConverter` wraps at `+` inside `sum({... for k in 1:m})`; `_bracketDepth` counts only `(...)` and `[...]`, not call parentheses, `{}` or if-branches. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: a term wrapped before `+` or `-` inside an if-expression or an array constructor, at any depth, is now a level further in than the statement's own continuation. A call's parentheses alone do not count - counting them reached every assert message (303 files) and put one message's continuation lines at two columns - and counting these constructs in `_bracketDepth` would have stopped the wrap and undone B470. Over MSL and Buildings, 54 files change (34 MSL, 20 Buildings), 104 lines each two spaces right and nothing else. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests.ATermWrappedInsideAnIfExpressionOrAnArrayIsALevelInFromTheStatementsContinuation`. Where the wrap falls is B487. |
| B486 ✅ | **Three SVN working-copy test classes still pass silently without svn, and some SVN assertions check only for null** | Testing | ⭐ | S | Found doing B481. `SvnChangedFilesSinceTests`, `SvnRevisionControlSystemTests` and `SvnWorkingCopyPathTests` use `SvnWorkingCopyFixture` and still `return` without a working copy, so on CI (no svn) they count as passes having asserted nothing, where `SvnOperationsTests` now reports the same as skipped; switch them to `Assert.SkipUnless` and check them for data-condition returns as B481 did. Some assertions are weak too: `SvnOperationsTests.GetLogEntries_WithRealSvnWorkingCopy_UntilFilter_FiltersEntries` and `GetChangedFiles_..._AtKnownRevision` assert only not-null, where the per-run repository makes the answer known (an Until filter of an hour ago returns nothing). **Fixed 2026-09-27**: every class on `SvnWorkingCopyFixture` now calls its `RequireWorkingCopy()`, one `Assert.SkipUnless` shared with `SvnOperationsTests`, so without svn the 32 working-copy tests are reported as skipped (measured with svn off PATH and the CI filter: 593 passed, 32 skipped, 0 failed - 11 of them had counted as passes). The data-condition returns in `SvnChangedFilesSinceTests` are assertions, `ADeletedFileIsNotReported` now deletes a file, and the not-null-only assertions in `SvnOperationsTests` and `SvnRevisionControlSystemTests` assert what the per-run repository makes known (trunk revision 7, HEAD 10, an Until of an hour ago returns nothing, the latest trunk commit added `Models/TestModel.mo`, the exact branch list), held by constants on `SvnTestRepository`. Verified by mutating `GetChangedFilePathsSince`, `GetLogEntries` and `GetCurrentRevision`; no product defect found. |
| B487 ✅ | **Long if-expressions and positional arguments wrap mid-term, never at `else if` or between arguments** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | M | Found doing B485. The formatter breaks a long if-expression or call only at `+`/`-` where the line runs out, never at `else if`/`elseif`/`else` or between positional arguments, so MSL's PartialFriction and MassWithStopAndFriction (up to 128 characters), ZDiode and TemperatureDependentDensity keep branches split mid-term where their authors wrote an `else if` a line, and PolyphaseElectroMagneticConverter's `sum(...)` calls an argument a line. Break at the branch keywords and between positional arguments first, measured over MSL and Buildings. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: an if-expression in an equation or statement that does not fit where it starts now has each `elseif`/`else if`/`else` start a line, a level in from the statement's continuation (a term wrapped in such a branch a level in from its `else`), and a call's later positional argument that does not fit after its `,` but does on a line of its own starts one; only then does a `+` or `-` wrap. Nothing breaks inside a first argument B464 may still move, an array still on its opening line, a string over several lines, or an if-expression inside another's condition or `then`. Over MSL and Buildings, 321 files change (141 MSL, 180 Buildings), only in line breaks and indentation - 114 only in an assert whose message now starts a line, 94 only in if-expressions; lines over 100 in them 9,172 to 8,639. Held by `ContinuationIndentTests.ALongIfExpressionBreaksAtItsBranchesAndACallBetweenItsArguments`. Five shapes it left are B489. |
| B488 ✅ | **A test run installed `mlqt hook install`'s pre-commit hook into the MLQT repository itself** | Testing / CLI | ⭐⭐ | S | Found 2026-09-27 when it blocked B487's commit: `C:\Projects\MLQT\.git\hooks\pre-commit` (dated 2026-09-22, "installed by `mlqt hook install`") ran `mlqt check "C:/Projects/MLQT/MLQT.Cli.Tests/bin/Release/net10.0"` - not a library - so every commit staging a `.mo` file failed. Deleted by the user's decision. The path says a hook install ran with the CLI test binaries' directory as its library, and that directory is inside this repository, so finding the repository by walking up from the library reached the real `.git`. Find which test (or `HookCommandTests` path) can do that, make every hook test use a temp repository that is not under any other working copy, and make `hook install` refuse (or at least warn) when the library it is given is not a library. **Fixed 2026-09-27**: the likeliest source is the 2026-09-22 mutation run (the hook's date): a mutant dropping the path from `HookOptions` makes every hook test install into the current directory - the test output directory inside MLQT's working copy - and the repository found above it is MLQT's own. `hook install` now refuses, with exit 2, a path in which `LibraryDiscovery` (as `mlqt check` uses it) finds no library, and names the repository the hook would have gone into; a successful install names its repository too. Every `HookCommandTests` test runs under `StrayHookGuard`, which fails a test that writes a `pre-commit` hook into a repository enclosing the test run and removes it if mlqt wrote it, and `TempRepo` throws if its temp directory is inside another working copy. Held by `APathWithNoLibraryInItIsRefused_AndTheRefusalNamesTheRepository`, `InstallSaysWhichRepositoryTheHookWentInto` and `AnInstallIntoAGuardedRepositoryFailsTheTest_AndIsRemoved`. Hooks in a worktree are B490. |
| B489 | **Five formatter wrap shapes B487 left** | Parser / formatting | ⭐ | M | Found doing B487, listed not chased: (1) the first positional argument is never moved or broken before (Buildings gFunction's 116-character `...timeGeometric(tSho_min,`, multipoleThermalResistances' `(2,` then `3, ...`); (2) an if-expression's condition is never wrapped - no break at `and`/`or`, nothing inside parentheses (VerifyDifferenceThreePeriods, MassWithStopAndFriction); (3) wraps fall at the innermost call rather than between an outer call's arguments or at a top-level `+` (smoothLimit, Dissipation's `SMOOTH(...)` sums); (4) a broken if-expression is followed on the same line by the next positional argument (MSL Machines); (5) named-argument wrapping estimates length from `GetText()` without spaces, disagreeing with B487's estimates (ReferenceMoistAir `X=cat(1, X,` then `{1 - sum(X)}));`). Each measured over MSL and Buildings as B462-B487 were; some may not be worth their complexity. |
